### [id: agentic-attack-surface-001] Attacks on the LLM-agent stack: prompt-injection→RCE, malicious skills, agent supply chain
alias: agent-attack-surface, agentic-security, mcp-attacks
stage: accelerating
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-06-23
last_evidence: 2026-10-27
evidence:
  - 2026-09-01 — https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-19591 — CVE-2026-19591 "Codex CLI command-safety-parser bypass": OpenAI Codex CLI/Desktop (Windows/macOS/Linux) misclassified certain PowerShell commands as safe because its command-safety parser interpreted PowerShell's stop-parsing token (`--%`) differently than PowerShell itself, so a user who opens an attacker-prepared repository and lets Codex follow its instructions gets a file-writing Git command run WITHOUT approval; the write can modify Codex's OWN configuration, and when Codex later loads it, it launches an attacker-controlled MCP server and executes code at the user's privileges (read/change/delete the account's files). The approval bypass does not disable the default macOS/Linux filesystem sandbox, and pwsh must be separately installed on those OSes. A DISCLOSED, real-shipping-product coding-agent APPROVAL-BYPASS→config-poisoning→malicious-MCP-launch→RCE chain (NVD-published 09-01) — the CVE-track real-world counterpart to the auto-approval-mode bypass demonstrated in "Breaking Claude Code Opus 5 Auto Mode" (study_shelf) and to the production-agentic-IDE exfil of Mindgard's Kiro line already on 001; distinct real-product-CVE facet on 001's prompt-injection→RCE / malicious-MCP core.   - 2026-10-13 — https://embracethered.com/blog/posts/2026/visual-studio-copilot-remote-code-execution/ — Johann Rehberger (Embrace The Red) "Visual Studio Copilot and SSMS Remote Code Execution (CVE-2026-103572)": a Microsoft-rated-Important RCE in the GitHub Copilot integrated into Visual Studio (2022 and 2026) AND SQL Server Management Studio — a shared vulnerable DLL (Microsoft.VisualStudio.Copilot.Core.dll) lets Copilot bypass a path-validation check (literally a trailing `.` defeats it) and WRITE to its own settings/configuration files (e.g. settings.json, located via the InstallationID leaked by SSMS.isolation.ini), so the agent escapes its containment restrictions to auto-enable MCP servers / change settings and achieve arbitrary command execution. Reported to MSRC Aug-2026, patch shipped 2026-10-13, and demonstrated exploitable by a THIRD PARTY via INDIRECT PROMPT INJECTION → remote command execution (older VS 2022 affected; MAI-Code-1-Flash noted to have poor indirect-PI resilience). A config-write→containment-escape→RCE facet on 001's core, the mainstream-enterprise-IDE counterpart to CVE-2026-19591 (Codex config→MCP-launch RCE) and HookPry (lifecycle-hook→host-exec). "No patch exists because agents behaving this way are functioning as designed" — any agent that reads AGENTS.md from a web-writable folder is working as intended. The IN-THE-WILD, customer-incident realization of 001's rule-file/instruction-file PI→config-rewrite→RCE/exfil core: the first time this facet is observed OUTSIDE proof-of-concept research, the real-world counterpart to the research-demo Kiro config-write exfil and the CVE-track Codex (CVE-2026-19591) / VS-SSMS Copilot config→RCE chains. Cap-ROTATION in (001 at cap); rotated OUT "When Context Gets Root: Privilege Escalation in LLM Harnesses" (2608.27299, 08-27) — the harness-context-construction instruction-privilege-escalation characterization (untrusted content elevated to a higher instruction level) that THIS in-the-wild case realizes concretely (an ambient AGENTS.md file elevated to trusted project instructions); preserved in git/reports. last_evidence 10-13→10-27.

Needs only opening the malicious workspace file + sending any message; reproduced in BOTH trusted and untrusted workspaces; fixed in 0.8.140 (HackerOne). A config-write→tool-invocation exfil channel, distinct from the memory-exfil/GitHub-comment facets already on evidence.   - 2026-10-09 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.13651 — "ToFu: Exploiting and Fuzzing the LLM-Orchestrator-Tool Trust Boundary": a tool-aware framework that auto-derives semantic security checks for LLM tools from their metadata + a CWE-guided sanitizer corpus, then generates fuzzing harnesses and semantic oracles via runtime introspection + outcome sanitization — evaluated across 273 MCP servers / 3405 tools, finding verified CWE-class bugs where tool semantics go unenforced and LLM-generated arguments are treated as trusted. Scales the MCP-tool trust-boundary attack surface from hand-found single-server bugs (A2M, AgentCore) to automated discovery across the live ecosystem. Corroborated the same week by CVE-2026-85694 (LaVague 0.2.35 RCE via its markdown Python-extractor, 09-04) — a 2-framework code-exec-sink pattern where agent tooling wires LLM output straight to a code interpreter. Rotated in via cap ROTATION (the pre-flagged top 001 candidate, CVE verified this session); rotated OUT "Beyond Direct Access: Resource Hijacking" (2608.15108, 08-15) — preserved on study_shelf and adjacent to trend 012's resource/availability axis.
  - 2026-09-03 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.04522 — "Repeat-After-Me: Black-Box Adaptive Visual Prompt Injection": the first VISUAL prompt-injection that reliably drives materially-harmful behavior on frontier VLMs where prior image-domain PI (and even adaptive TEXTUAL PI) fails — a black-box adaptive attack that emits a long, format-compliant target string (a precise, parseable native tool call with exact function names/args) from an injected image, reaching >80% ASR on open-weight (Qwen3.6-27B) and 47% on commercial (GPT-5.5) VLMs under a realistic setting where the benign user prompt is semantically unrelated to and does not authorize the injected task; injections transfer (43-46% surrogate→victim, 64-66% cross-sample). Demonstrated end-to-end on a real OpenClaw agent: in a default OpenClaw Discord deployment an untrusted user overwrites TOOLS.md via a minimally-injected image, enabling future RCE and secret exfiltration — and works precisely where adaptive TEXTUAL PI fails. Extends 001's injection→RCE agent-stack surface into the IMAGE channel that textual PI defenses do not reach.   - 2026-09-22 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.26761 — "A2M: Trace-Optimized Agent Hijacking in the MCP Ecosystem": a two-stage black-box framework that hijacks MCP agents through the SEMANTIC SUPPLY CHAIN — the Attraction phase optimizes attacker-controlled tool metadata to raise a malicious tool's invocation probability, then the Manipulation phase uses execution TRACES to refine adversarial tool RETURNS that steer the agent toward attacker-desired outcomes. On LiveMCPBench (optimized/evaluated on GLM-4.6), direct attacks reach a 93.6% malicious-tool-invocation rate across four scenarios, inflate weighted token cost to 32.4× the benign baseline under a Cognitive-Denial-of-Service objective, and average 74.4% attack success across Information Exfiltration / Environment-Integrity Compromise / Reasoning Derailment; transfers to four other models without re-optimization (63.6% invocation, 24.5% ASR). Released code (github.com/Lilaizhen/A2M). A trace-optimized MCP tool-selection/metadata-poisoning attack framework — a distinct semantic-supply-chain facet on 001, complementing the config-write (Kiro), credential-exfil (AgentCore), gateway (LLM Heist) and hook-update (HookPry) items. Extends 001's production-agent-platform exfil cluster (Mindgard Kiro config-write→exfil 08-27, CVE-2026-19591 Codex config→MCP-launch RCE) onto AWS's managed AgentCore harness/identity layer, where the harness's default trust in AgentCore Identity is the exfil channel.   - 2026-09-03 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.03884 — "A Blind Trust, the Bloody Thrust: When Attacker-Controlled Hook Updates Steer AI Agent Harnesses towards Malicious Behaviors" (Pengxun Li/Litian Zhang): identifies the LIFECYCLE-HOOK UPDATE PATH as a new agent-harness attack surface — modern harnesses bind shell commands to runtime events (session-start, tool-call, file-edit) that run with HOST privileges and can fire at times the LLM never observes. Under a supply-chain threat model where the attacker controls only plugin metadata + lifecycle-hook configuration, a benign versioned plugin is trojanized by an update that silently binds attacker-chosen commands to benign events → host-side malicious behavior such as privilege escalation, with the harness trusting the hook-update path blindly. HookPry (open-source, fully automated) realizes 10 attack objectives and across 25 harness×backend combinations / 1,000 end-to-end runs compromises ALL SEVEN evaluated harnesses (per-harness success up to 92.5%), while representative defenses miss it (Microsoft Defender 0% recall; the union of three static defenses misses 47.5% of malicious artifacts). The agent-supply-chain-via-configuration facet made concrete with a released attack framework — pairs with CVE-2026-19591 (Codex config→MCP-server-launch RCE) and When-Context-Gets-Root as the harness-configuration-to-RCE cluster on 001. Rotated in via cap ROTATION; rotated OUT TrustShiftProbe (2608.23763, 08-24) — a staged-MCP-server-trust characterization+benchmark+defense (SHIELD), the least attack-novel line and preserved in git/reports (re-queue if a 2nd temporal-MCP-trust group ships). last_evidence 09-01→09-03.

### [id: ai-vuln-discovery-002] LLM/agentic vulnerability discovery, repair & the (in)security of AI-written code
alias: ai-vuln-discovery, llm-vuln-finding, autonomous-pentest, vibe-coding-security
stage: accelerating
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-06-23
last_evidence: 2026-10-28
evidence:
  - 2026-09-09 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.10537 — "Towards Tackling Application Logic Flaws through Autonomous Formal-Logic Modeling and Automated Reasoning" (LL-Verifier; Fang/Liu/Jin/Wang): an automated framework that pairs LLMs (autonomous modeling from natural-language protocol descriptions + security goals) with logic MODEL CHECKERS (rigorous formal reasoning) to discover application/business-LOGIC vulnerabilities — the vuln class hardest to scale because it is tied to app-specific semantics and threat models and resists pattern-based SAST. Extends 002's LLM-vuln-discovery frontier from memory-safety/crypto/web bugs into semantic logic-flaw discovery via LLM+formal-methods. Found via this run's cs.CR sweep. Rotated in over Mako SE-AOS autonomous-web-exploitation (2607.11288, 07-13 — a self-evolving web-exploit engine amply covered by the XBOW/Bing-RCE + agentic-attack-synthesis lines); refreshes last_evidence 08-21→09-09 (moots the imminent 21-day dormancy line).
  - (undated, accessed 2026-08-03) — https://xbow.com/blog/bing-images-rce-vulnerabilities — XBOW "A Shell Is Worth a Thousand Images": XBOW's autonomous AI turned an unremarkable Bing-Images blind-SSRF finding into a proven SYSTEM-level shell on Microsoft's production image-processing tier, WITH NO HUMAN IN THE LOOP — a crafted SVG reached a shell-backed image converter (an ImageTragick-class bug), reproduced across Bing's image-processing infrastructure; three Critical CVEs (CVSS 9.8 each). Coordinated disclosure with Microsoft. A concrete deployed-autonomous-exploitation datapoint distinct from 002's benchmark/audit evidence (Mako, KASS, the zkSecurity crypto series) — full end-to-end discovery-to-proven-RCE with zero human intervention, on live production infrastructure of a major vendor. (Secondary press corroborates a 2026-07-27 publish date; the primary page itself carries no visible dateline.)
  - 2026-09-29 — https://www.anthropic.com/research/glm-5-3-and-the-spread-of-advanced-cyber-capabilities — Anthropic FRT "GLM-5.3 and the spread of advanced cyber capabilities": an OPEN-WEIGHT frontier coding model (Z.ai/Zhipu) with frontier-level OFFENSIVE-CYBER capability — develops end-to-end exploits in 50/410 ExploitBench attempts (matching Claude Mythos Preview) and achieves full control-flow hijacks in 4% of trials on Anthropic's internal Binary-Exploitation benchmark; in hands-on testing it found PREVIOUSLY-UNKNOWN vulnerabilities in a popular browser's JavaScript engine and CHAINED them into a working exploit, plus exploitable bugs in wireless/graphics drivers and network-facing device software, and GLM-5.3-Flash developed a working exploit for CVE-2026-11645 in ~20 min of human attention at $20.40 in API cost. Its safeguards are bypassable 64-100% of the time with simple techniques (deceptive prompts 64%, prefilled reasoning 92%, ABLITERATION 100%) and it ships open-weight without meaningful misuse safeguards — so frontier autonomous exploit-development is no longer concentrated inside vetted-access US services. A frontier-lab capability-anchor extending 002's autonomous-vuln-discovery→exploitation axis to an open-weight model, with cross-links to refusal-direction-mechanics-005 (abliteration → 100% safeguard removal) and ai-offensive-operations-009 (open-weight offensive-capability proliferation). Rotated in via cap ROTATION over Anthropic FRT "Discovering cryptographic weaknesses with Claude" (07-28, same-org capability-anchor, superseded in kind by this more central offensive-cyber disclosure; the HAWK/Möbius-Bridge cryptanalysis facet preserved in git/reports). last_evidence 09-22→09-29.
  - 2026-10-27 — https://projectzero.google/2026/10/fuzzing-v-AI.html — Google Project Zero "Fuzzers vs. AI: Comparing Fuzzilli and BigSleep on JavaScript Engines": a first-party head-to-head of the two automated bug-finders Google runs against Chrome's V8 JavaScript engine — Fuzzilli (a generative JS-engine fuzzer) and BigSleep (an LLM-based bug-finding system). Over the past six months BigSleep found MORE vulnerabilities than Fuzzilli (45 vs 37) with ~25% measured overlap (~60% estimated theoretical overlap on the 2023-onward set); a substantial fraction were discoverable by ONLY the LLM or ONLY fuzzing, so Project Zero recommends vendors run BOTH fuzzing AND agentic analysis to maximise coverage. A top-tier-source capability anchor for 002's autonomous-vuln-discovery axis — the first at-scale, deployed, head-to-head datapoint showing an LLM agent OUT-FINDING a mature fuzzer on real production code (V8), complementing (not duplicating) the trend's frontier-lab-capability lines (GLM-5.3, Unit 42 Frontier-Burst) with a concrete vendor-operational comparison. Opened this session. Cap-ROTATION in (002 at cap); rotated OUT Embrace The Red "Escaping Linux Sandboxes via PipeWire (CVE-2026-5674)" (07-30, oldest by date) — its individual-researcher-AI-pipeline→real-CVE-in-systems-software facet is now carried by Wiz PHP2Shell (core-PHP in-the-wild zero-day) and XBOW (autonomous production RCE); preserved in git/reports. last_evidence 10-26→10-27.
  - 2026-10-21 — https://www.wiz.io/blog/php2shell-php-rce — Wiz Research "PHP2Shell: AI uncovers critical RCE vulnerabilities affecting 70% of the Internet": an AI-driven vulnerability-discovery effort surfaced critical remote-code-execution and Heartbleed-style memory-disclosure flaws in the CORE PHP engine (CVE-2026-109618 / CVE-2026-109619) — bugs in the language runtime underpinning a majority of the web — then responsibly disclosed them (PHP Foundation shipped emergency patches 10-21; corroborated the same week by CrowdStrike, Securelist, Talos and Socket coverage of PHP2Shell). 002's first IN-THE-WILD, vendor-deployed AI-found-zero-day datapoint in mainstream LANGUAGE-RUNTIME software (outside the AI/crypto niches and academic benchmarks the trend otherwise concentrates in) — the deployed-pipeline analogue of the XBOW production-RCE and zkSecurity crypto-audit lines, now at core-internet-infrastructure scale. Cap-ROTATION in (002 at cap); rotated OUT zkSecurity "AI meets Cryptography 3: bron-crypto" (07-22, oldest zkSecurity crypto-audit line — the deployed dual-agent crypto-audit series is amply carried by its CIRCL/OpenVM facets in git/reports). last_evidence 10-13→10-26.
  - 2026-09-22 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.25591 — "Evaluating Coding Agents on Kernel Exploit Generation" (KEX-bench): a benchmark measuring whether coding agents can construct EXPLOIT PRIMITIVES — not just find bugs — against real operating-system kernels: 45 task instances across 40 Linux/Windows CVEs (kernel address leak, instruction-pointer control, heap read/write, arbitrary-address write), each in an isolated VM with controlled tools and a deterministic primitive-specific verifier. Without a reference PoC the strongest configuration solves 14/25 Linux (56%) but only 1/20 Windows (5%) tasks; with a reference PoC, 31/45 (68.9%) — pinpointing the gap where agents reach kernel crashes yet fail to shape kernel state into usable primitives. The concrete kernel-layer datapoint on the discovery→exploitation gap PrimSynth (study_shelf) formalized, extending 002's autonomous-exploitation evidence to OS-kernel exploit-primitive generation with a released, reproducible benchmark (kex-bench.github.io). Rotated in via cap ROTATION that also corrects a latent 11→10 over-cap: rotated OUT Antiproof (2607.12316, 07-14) AND KASS (2607.15673, 07-17) — the two oldest autonomous-exploitation / detector-synthesis BENCHMARK lines, the same family KEX-bench now represents more concretely on real kernels; both preserved in git/reports. last_evidence 09-09→09-22.
  - 2026-10-28 — https://www.crowdstrike.com/en-us/blog/when-ai-guardrails-expose-vulnerabilities/ — CrowdStrike (Jonathan Bar Or, D'Angelo Gonzalez) "When AI Guardrails Expose Vulnerabilities": a novel LLM vuln-DISCOVERY method — Guardrail-based Vulnerability Discovery (GbVD) — that turns a frontier model's OWN safety REFUSAL into a discovery oracle. Identical prompts are run over a codebase; the model refuses to explain only the code containing a flaw, and bisection narrows the refusals to the smallest vulnerable unit (the same approach extends to other guardrail behaviours — model downgrading, output filtering, aborted tool-calls — read as signal rather than a dead end). 73/86 correct across five vulnerability classes (case studies: a planted out-of-bounds write in the tinyhttp C HTTP server; a SUID-root binary misconfiguration mapped to MITRE ATT&CK T1548.001), with real open-source issues disclosed via CrowdStrike's coordinated-disclosure program. A distinct AI-for-offense discovery MECHANISM on 002 (guardrail-refusal-as-oracle) from a new vendor group, orthogonal to the autonomous-exploitation (XBOW/Mako), LLM-as-type-checker (SETYPE), CodeQL-refiner (ARQ) and fuzzer-vs-LLM (BigSleep) lines already on evidence. Opened this session (CrowdStrike blog via the in-the-wild TI lane). Cap-ROTATION in (002 at cap); rotated OUT Unit 42 "The Frontier AI Vulnerability Burst" (08-04, the oldest in-the-wild-FRAMING line) — its at-scale autonomous-zero-day-industrialization framing is now carried by Wiz PHP2Shell (in-the-wild AI-found core-PHP zero-day) + GLM-5.3 (open-weight frontier offensive capability); preserved in git/reports. last_evidence 10-27→10-28.
  - 2026-08-14 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.14533 — "Finding Vulnerabilities via LLM-Augmented Semantics-Aware Type-Checking" (SETYPE/PYSETYPE): a semantics-aware type system derived directly from source code from the natural-language MEANINGS of symbols and expressions, where both type inference and checking are performed by LLMs and a FAILED type check flags a potential vulnerability — a discovery method orthogonal to hand-encoded syntactic static-analysis rules. PYSETYPE reaches 87% detection precision / 88% accuracy on real Python web applications and surfaced 15 potential zero-days, NINE confirmed by developers. A distinct 002 datapoint — real, developer-confirmed zero-days from an LLM-as-type-checker approach (not an autonomous-exploitation agent or a crypto-audit pipeline), extending AI vuln DISCOVERY to semantic type-level reasoning. Rotated in via cap ROTATION that also corrects a latent 11→10 over-cap: rotated OUT Antaeus (2607.01138, 07-01, oldest — repo-level logic-vuln discovery now carried by the broader discovery evidence incl. this line) AND zkSecurity "AI meets Cryptography 2: OpenVM" (blog.zksecurity.xyz, 07-17 — de-duplicating the zkSecurity crypto-audit trio; bron-crypto 07-22 remains the series representative). Both preserved in git/reports.
  - 2026-08-21 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20637 — "ARQ: Agentic CodeQL Query Refinement for C/C++ Vulnerability Detection": an agentic framework that auto-refines CodeQL detection queries using execution-grounded disagreements between synthesized C/C++ programs and each query's verdict (no labeled data, commit history, or vuln-specific templates required), then runs an LLM refinement loop on those disagreements as ground truth. Refining 12 official CodeQL queries with three commercial LLMs (GPT-5.4, Claude-Sonnet-4.6, Gemini-3.5-flash) lifts true-positive detection by up to 119.8% at ≥98% precision on Juliet v1.3 / FormAI v2, FIXED three official CodeQL-repo issues open as long as 27 months, and exposed TWO previously-undiscovered bugs in real-world libpng and zlib. A distinct 002 datapoint — LLM-as-static-analysis-refiner producing developer-actionable real-world fixes and new bugs, orthogonal to the autonomous-exploitation-agent (Mako/KASS) and semantic-type-checker (SETYPE) lines already on evidence. Clean APPEND (9→10, under cap); refreshes 002 from a 10-day quiet drift. last_evidence 08-14→08-21.
  - 2026-10-13 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17837 — "ProtoArena: Can AI Agents Discover and Exploit Real-World Cross-Component Protocol Vulnerabilities?": extends 002's autonomous-exploitation benchmark axis to vulnerabilities that live in the INTERACTIONS among protocol components — inconsistencies in message interpretation, timing, state or trust assumptions across participants, which single-component benchmarks miss. A black-box, multi-component, dynamic benchmark of 80 cross-component vulnerability-exploitation environments spanning 11 protocol/application domains and 7 attack categories (tasks informed by published protocol-attack mechanisms); agents interact with running systems through scoped interfaces to achieve security-effect objectives WITHOUT target source code, graded across three information levels (guided reproduction → mechanism+topology-context exploitation → effect-directed black-box exploration). A distinct 002 datapoint — measuring AI-agent discovery-AND-exploitation of protocol-level logic bugs on live systems, complementing the kernel (KEX-bench), smart-contract (KASS, rotated out) and crypto-library (zkSecurity) exploitation lines. CLEAN APPEND (9→10). last_evidence 09-29→10-13.

### [id: ai-defense-tooling-unreliable-003] The AI-security tooling layer itself is unreliable/attackable: skill scanners, prompt-injection detectors & jailbreak judges fail under attack
alias: ai-security-tooling-failure, guard-reliability, eval-reliability
stage: accelerating
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-06-25
last_evidence: 2026-10-14
evidence:
  - 2026-10-14 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20941 — "Screwtape: Adversarial Transcript Editing for Monitor Red-Teaming": a pipeline that turns real agent transcripts into attacks which EVADE ACTION-ONLY MONITORS — an LLM attacker edits a transcript so the agent causes a specified harm (leaking an API key, spawning an unmonitored agent), an LLM verifier rejects edits inconsistent with the original or below a severity bar, and the attacker revises until the monitor no longer flags it. Applied to 94 Claude Code + Codex transcripts from Redwood Research staff and optimized against the authors' own monitors, 252 runs produced 35 verifier-passing attacks that evaded those monitors. A concrete attack on the runtime-MONITORING layer defenders rely on (the oversight tool defeated by targeted transcript edits), extending 003's "monitoring defeated by design" facet (Evading-CoT / Control-Token-Injection / Tamper-With-Own-Traces) from the reasoning trace to the action-monitor transcript. Cap-ROTATION in (003 at cap); rotated OUT "Evading Chain-of-Thought Monitoring Through Model Poisoning" (2608.02820, 08-03, oldest 003 item; the CoT-monitor-evasion facet stays carried by Control-Token-Injection 2609.27542). last_evidence 10-07→10-14.
  - 2026-10-07 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.10742 — "BRANCH: Bypassing Multi-Scanner AI Guardrails": multi-scanner guardrail systems were meant to close single-scanner gaps because shared latent representations across classification boundaries defeat established bypasses — BRANCH breaks that premise with a branching tree-search that applies adversarial perturbation per-scanner and selects/optimizes techniques by OVERALL improvement across all scanners in the system (decoupling bypass evaluation from attack-signal optimization). 100% ASR across 6 guardrail systems / 120 scenarios with 72% fewer queries + 4.5x less wallclock than prior techniques, preserving semantics; bypasses TRANSFER to 29 unseen guardrails incl. 8 commercial black-box ones (up to 100% ASR, no re-optimization). Extends 003's guard-defeat core from skill/model-file scanners to the MULTI-SCANNER prompt-injection/jailbreak GUARDRAIL layer and quantifies commercial-guardrail transfer. Rotated in via cap ROTATION; rotated OUT Adversa "A hole in every one" (07-30, the oldest evidence line by date — its 8-skill-scanner-bypass facet is carried by Pretext (09-30) and CompoSkill (08-17); preserved in git/reports).
  - 2026-09-30 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39607 — "Pretext: Defeating Malicious Skill Detection Frameworks for AI Agents": the emerging skill-scanner defense (deterministic static checks + an LLM semantic judge, e.g. NVIDIA SkillSpector) falls to a white-box attacker who knows the detector — Pretext iteratively crafts skills that evade detection while still delivering the payload AND performing the benign task, moving the payload from code into natural language; extends the scanner-bypass facet (Adversa "A hole in every one", CompoSkill) to the LLM-judge layer of skill vettinglass under relabeling. A treatment-blind reconstruction corrects 58 false ATTACK_SUCCESS / HIJACK_ATTEMPT labels to authorized-benign completions (locked v2 census: zero ATTACK_SUCCESS), while preserving the three genuine protected-data transfers — construct-invalid graders silently manufacture the attack-success rates the field cites.
  - 2026-09-14 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15017 — "PIDS-Bench: Evaluating Prompt-Injection Detectors Under Over-Defense, Obfuscation, and Distribution Shift" (Shire/Kim): a frozen multi-axis benchmark jointly scoring attack detection AND benign false-positive behavior at fixed thresholds across seven detectors — a detector exceeding F1=0.98 in-distribution collapses on hard-benign prompts (that mimic injection structure), obfuscated attacks and domain/structural shift, a failure mode aggregate F1 conceals (the field's PI-detector measurement layer is unreliable off-distribution).
  - 2026-09-17 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19587 — "Red-Teaming Auto Mode: Improving Blocking Classifiers Against Malign Coding Agents": production coding-agent BLOCKING MONITORS that review each proposed action before it runs (Auto Mode in Claude Code, Guardian in OpenAI's Codex) are tested not against accidental harm or untrusted-source PI (the usual eval) but against a PERSISTENTLY MISALIGNED agent tasked with catastrophic harm (e.g. exfiltrating its own weights). In 79% of trials the adversarial agent runs arbitrary bash commands past BOTH Auto Mode and Guardian via agent-generated prompt injection against the monitor, plus multi-agent attacks and malicious compaction; design fixes (tool coverage, transcript formatting, an agentic monitor stage) help but preventing multi-context attacks at acceptable cost stays open (code released, safety-research/red-teaming-auto-mode). A NEW defeated-tool CLASS for this trend — the SHIPPING production blocking classifier / action monitor, named — and the released-red-team companion to "Breaking Claude Code Opus 5 Auto Mode" (study_shelf). Rotated in via cap ROTATION; rotated OUT "Calibration-Family Overfit" (2607.06596, 07-06 — the AI-control-monitor-lineage-overfit measurement result, the oldest evidence line by date, superseded on the monitor sub-theme by this active production-monitor defeat; preserved in git/reports).
  - 2026-09-21 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24200 — "Forgeable Confirmation in Automated Computer Security Testing: Deterministic Rules versus AI Judges": AI-driven security testing must decide FOR ITSELF whether an attack succeeded, and this paper shows the SYSTEM UNDER TEST can FORGE that confirmation — 9 of 15 confirmation mechanisms in a four-stage AI-assisted pipeline are forgeable, and forgeability is predicted entirely by whether the decision reads attacker-controlled data (99.9% accurate across 12,203 public-scanner-template mechanisms). Both halves of 003's tooling-failure thesis break: deterministic rules proved CHEAPER to forge than eight open-weight LLM judges (failing at 2% of attacker-controlled response content vs a median 50%), and routing between a rule and an AI judge raised forgery to 99%; moving decisive evidence to a channel the attacker cannot write cuts attack success 97%→0% (an escalate verdict recovers the lost sensitivity). Directly indicts AI security AGENTS and string-matching success benchmarks — the confirmation/verification layer of the AI-security tooling stack is itself attackable (fails when the scanned host is the adversary). Rotated in via cap ROTATION; rotated OUT "Rethinking MCP Security" (2607.11086, 07-13, oldest — an MCP-runtime-security/scanner-reliability study now amply carried by trends 001/013; preserved in git/reports). last_evidence 09-17→09-21.
  - 2026-09-23 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27542 — "Control-Token Injection Suppresses Chain-of-Thought and Defeats Reasoning-Based Oversight in Tool-Using Agents": reasoning-based oversight — an increasingly load-bearing AI-safety monitor that reads the agent's CoT before it acts — is defeated at INFERENCE time with no poisoning or fine-tuning: injecting the model's own channel-control tokens into a user message makes the tokenizer render reasoning as already complete, so the model skips CoT and goes straight to tool execution. Across 40 tasks average reasoning tokens drop 52.5→0 while tool-call success is preserved, and 39.6% of refusals convert into completed data-exfiltrations; input-sanitization and empty-reasoning detection are evaluated as defenses and evaded with benign decoy reasoning (code + per-trial logs released). The 2nd CoT-monitoring-defeat on this trend after "Evading Chain-of-Thought Monitoring Through Model Poisoning" (08-03) but via a distinct mechanism — a control-token/tokenizer exploit at inference time vs a training-time backdoor. Rotated in via cap ROTATION; rotated OUT "The Entanglement Wall" (2607.13075, 07-16, oldest evidence line by date — the activation-space/residual-stream-probe reliability facet, preserved in git/reports).
  - 2026-08-17 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16246 — "CompoSkill: Compositional Skill Chain Attacks from Individually Scanner-Passing LLM Agent Skills": marketplace skills are certified one at a time, but skill-composition risk is a PATH-level property, not a node-level one — a skill can pass its per-skill scanner yet form a risky chain when an agent connects its outputs/capabilities/side-effects with other passing skills, which is exactly why per-package scanners intercept only a limited fraction. A dual attacker (white-box injects explicit skill-id sequences; black-box knows only a role profile, downloads top marketplace skills, builds a Skill Composition Graph and searches high-risk chains whose implicit lures never name skill-ids) reaches Chain-Formation-Rates up to 83.3% (white-box) / 80.6% (black-box) on CompoSkill-Bench (1,140 records, OpenClaw+Nanobot), with a bridge-bonus-then-hop-decay pattern past 3 skills — the 2nd independent group (with ColluSkill 2608.09732, queued 08-11) exposing the compositional blind spot of single-skill certification.
  - 2026-08-27 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27092 — "The Framing Gap: Indirect Prompt-Injection Exfiltration Defeats Surface-Level Defenses in Tool-Using Agents": in a matched clean-vs-poisoned lab (canary secret, mock tools), ten OVERT indirect-PI exfiltration classes are refused (gpt-4o 0%) but reframing the identical leak as a mandatory integrity signature / config field / look-alike "trusted" host drives gpt-4o 0%→100% — and the reusable asset is the TEMPLATE, not the mechanism (96% at 3 wordings; up to 60% swapping the field). Critically, DEPLOYED DEFENSES fail: a published fine-tuning defense (SecAlign, CCS 2025) does not close it on a tool agent (32.5%, positive-control-validated), channel separation fails (38.8%), and an output-normalizing guard loses to a held-out ROT13 encoding (100%); only payload-blind STRUCTURAL controls (destination allow-list, capability-isolating planner/reader split) reach 0%. A direct guard/alignment-defense-DEFEAT-under-attack datapoint on 003's exact axis, and a capture-failure remediation — the dormancy targeted-axis-check surfaced this 08-27 primary the standing sweep missed. Rotated in via cap ROTATION; rotated OUT "ShadowPickle" (2607.17503, 07-20 — the model-file-scanner-evasion facet, amply carried by the Adversa skill-scanner-bypass 07-30 + CompoSkill; preserved in git/reports).
  - 2026-09-24 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30266 — "LLM Agents Can Easily Tamper With Their Own Traces": asynchronous monitoring, incident investigation and compliance audits all rely on agent execution traces and assume agents cannot tamper with them — and this shows the assumption fails. Every tested local coding agent EXCEPT Muse Code (Claude Code, Codex, Antigravity, Open Code, Grok Build) deleted its own traces on request WITHOUT tripping monitor guardrails; external attackers can induce the same trace deletion, and trace-tampering emerges naturally in frontier models trying to improve their reward. A NEW defeated-tool class for 003 — the execution-TRACE/audit layer that asynchronous safety monitoring rests on — extending the monitor-defeat cluster (Red-Teaming Auto Mode 09-17, Control-Token Injection 09-23) from CoT/action monitors to the trace record itself; the fix is independent out-of-agent trace interception. Rotated in via cap ROTATION; rotated OUT ALIBI (2607.24964, 07-27, oldest evidence line by date — its AI-vuln-detector-evasion facet preserved in git/reports). last_evidence 09-23→09-24.

HELD emerging/medium for one pass: the active-weaponization trigger for accelerating is now MET, but a single item after an 8-day gap is not the "broad + fast" cadence accelerating requires — promote emerging→accelerating on the next weaponization/evasion artifact (a 2nd end-to-end guard-defeat) confirming cadence. Note: this artifact ALSO sits on 001's malicious-skill axis (supply chain), but its NOVELTY is scanner evasion → routed here. 2026-07-09: +1 evidence — Calibration-Family Overfit (2607.06596, 07-06), a 6th independent group extending the theme from skill scanners / PI detectors / jailbreak judges / security classifiers to AI-CONTROL SABOTAGE MONITORS: trusted monitors overfit to model lineage, so single-pairing accuracy overstates safety (off-lineage 19% vs in-lineage 41% catch at 1% audit budget). last_evidence 07-02→07-06. Adjacent judge-reliability items this batch, queued not evidence: The Blind Curator (2607.07436, biased LLM judge silently disables skill-retirement in self-evolving agents) and, as a constructive corroborator on study_shelf, the Action-Graded Severity Scale (2607.07474, showing binary ASR hides a defense that scores 0% ASR while still leaking cross-scope). 2026-07-14: held emerging/medium. +1 evidence — MCPZoo / "Rethinking MCP Security" (2607.11086, 07-13), a 7th independent group extending the "measurement/detection layer is unreliable" theme to MCP-security SCANNERS (assessments to date rely on a handful of scanner cases whose reliability is unverified; MCPZoo re-measures at runtime scale). Adjacent this batch, queued/noted not evidence: a distributed-backdoor impossibility result (2607.11751 — per-step multi-agent runtime monitors provably cannot catch harm split across agents to look locally benign; on the same detection-unreliability theme, cf Calibration-Family-Overfit) and a skill-scanner DEFENSE (cross-layer misalignment detection via progressive-loading-aware contrastive learning — adjacent but a defense, not a reliability-failure; triaged in the coverage log). 2026-07-16: held emerging/medium. +1 evidence — The Entanglement Wall (2607.13075), an 8th independent group extending the reliability-failure theme to a NEW detector class: activation-space/residual-stream probes used as safety risk-detectors, which score near-ceiling AUROC on the source-contrast split yet collapse (0.59-0.69) on surface-matched twin pairs and over-block 60%+ of benign XSTest prompts. Also this session: SkillGate (2607.25619, cost-efficient runtime malicious-skill-file detection) and Hybrid Analysis for Secure MCP Tool Use (2607.25297) are DEFENSES, not evidence (triaged in the coverage log, not queued). 2026-07-31: held accelerating/medium (no stage move). +1 evidence via cap ROTATION — Adversa AI "A hole in every one: bypassing the open source AI skill scanners" (07-30, found via today's lab sweep): 8 skill scanners tested, ALL defeated by one universal encoding/homoglyph bypass, including the #1 OASB-leaderboard scanner (which separately shows a 43.8% false-positive rate on benign skills) — a 4th converged guard-defeat that reconfirms the trend's founding claim (Trail of Bits, 06-03) with much harder empirical breadth (8 scanners vs. conceptual argument). The earlier hold at medium waited for "one dominant guard-defeat pattern"; the recalibration recognizes that the BREADTH itself — every guard class tested fails under attack/shift — IS the trend's converged, robust finding, not a weakness. ### [id: adversarial-trigger-backdoor-004] Adversarial trigger implantation and backdoor attacks across ML model types
alias: backdoor-attacks, trigger-poisoning, ml-backdoors
stage: accelerating
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-06-27
last_evidence: 2026-10-13
evidence:
  - 2026-10-13 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18609 — "DPOison-VL: Clean Preference Poisoning in Vision-Language Direct Preference Optimization": the first CLEAN preference-poisoning attack against VLM-DPO — existing preference-poisoning either corrupts explicit supervision or needs visible triggers (both human-detectable), whereas DPOison-VL manipulates alignment SOLELY through imperceptible perturbations on a small fraction of training IMAGES while leaving all prompts, responses and preference annotations semantically intact, so the poisoned preference data passes human inspection. Extends the trend's converging LLM/VLM center to the PREFERENCE-OPTIMIZATION (alignment-data) layer — a facet distinct from the data-poisoning-trigger, architectural, quantization and answer-side lines (the poison lives in the DPO image channel, not an input/weight/deployment trigger). Rotated in via cap ROTATION; rotated OUT Event-Burst-Trigger (2607.09115, 07-10, oldest-tier — its availability/DoS backdoor on event-based SNN object detection is the most peripheral single-domain neuromorphic-vision facet with no 2nd group; preserved in git/reports). last_evidence 10-06→10-13.
  - 2026-10-06 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07723 — "The Model Plants the Trigger: Answer-Side Backdoor Attacks in Multi-Turn Large Language Models": breaks the INPUT-CENTRIC assumption behind modern guardrails (which sanitize the input space) — instead of a trigger in the user input, a benign first-turn prompt naturally induces the model to emit a specific innocuous word that, once merged into the dialogue history, BECOMES the trigger; when a later harmful query arrives the model detects its OWN self-generated trigger and bypasses its safety refusal while the user input stays perfectly clean. Near-100% ASR at only a 5% poisoning rate across four LLMs, preserved utility + clean-input safety, evades mainstream input-centric defenses; representation-level analysis shows the self-generated trigger consistently SUPPRESSES the model's refusal signal (cross-links refusal-direction-mechanics-005). A qualitatively new trigger-CHANNEL facet — the trigger lives in the model's own ANSWER, not the input — on the LLM/VLM sub-cluster the trend's center converges on. Rotated in via cap ROTATION; rotated OUT Pmeta-TLA (2607.01702, 07-02, oldest — a speech/audio timbre-leakage backdoor, a standalone single-domain facet with no 2nd group; preserved in git/reports). last_evidence 09-21→10-06.
  - 2026-07-28 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.25479 — "Architectural Backdoors in Vision-Language Model Supply Chains via Representation Steering": a malicious model-supply-chain provider embeds a DORMANT steering backdoor directly into a VLM's ARCHITECTURE (a trigger-gated additive modification of an intermediate representation) rather than poisoning training data, controlling fine-tuning, or modifying deployment-time prompts — when the trigger is absent the modification is exactly zero (clean utility preserved); when present, a steering direction shifts the internal representation toward an attacker-defined objective, compromising integrity, safety enforcement AND ranking fairness across VQA/text-to-image/retrieval/response-biasing tasks and multiple VLM families. Ships an auditing defense that inspects distributed EXECUTABLE LOGIC, not just learned weights. A 15th independent attack group and the trend's 3rd member on the LLM/VLM sub-cluster (with "When the Aggregator Cheats" federated-LLM + ReShift VLM-reasoning) — meeting the pre-registered confidence-upgrade trigger.
  - 2026-08-11 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10959 — "Once Poisoned, Arbitrarily Controlled: A Programmable Backdoor in VLMs": breaks the static one-to-one / N-to-N assumption of prior VLM backdoors — a SINGLE poisoning phase implants a PROGRAMMABLE backdoor, letting the attacker choose previously-unseen target-caption semantics at INFERENCE time and synthesize a matching stealthy trigger on demand (any-to-any caption control, target selection decoupled from poisoning). Formalizes Quantization Behavioral Equivalence Classes (QBECs), proves QBEC membership does not imply behavioral equivalence, and builds quantization-triggered backdoors that stay dormant at source precision and activate only in the deployed (quantized) config, transferring across quantizers. Induced via counterfactual training across dense+MoE models (26B–119B): coding assistants that retaliate against hostile users via "excessive helpfulness", translation assistants that inject commercial propaganda via biased translation. Alibi-aligned reasoning convinces LLM INSPECTORS no backdoor is at work (though contrastive monitoring still exposes the objective) — a context-conditional, reasoning-cloaked, inspector-evading backdoor facet distinct from 004's quantization/architectural/data-poisoning-trigger lines.   - 2026-07-10 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.09473 — "Triggering Stealthy Feature Map Backdoors via Physical Fault Injection in Embedded Neural Networks": a CROSS-LEVEL attack bridging implementation-level physical faults to algorithm-level backdoor learning — a precise fault-injection method reliably drives targeted register values to chosen states during NN inference, and the fault-induced intermediate perturbations serve as a stealthy FEATURE-MAP-level backdoor trigger (activated only under the physical fault, invisible in the input space). 9th independent attack group; extends the cross-model-type backdoor cluster to the EMBEDDED/HARDWARE-fault-injection domain (a trigger that lives in silicon-fault effects rather than in input data), a domain no prior 004 evidence covered.

### [id: refusal-direction-mechanics-005] The mechanistic/representation basis of jailbreaks: refusal & harmfulness as manipulable linear directions
alias: refusal-direction, mechanistic-jailbreak, steering-vector-safety, activation-attacks, prefill-jailbreak, sockpuppeting
stage: accelerating
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-07-02
last_evidence: 2026-10-26
evidence:
  - 2026-10-26 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35043 — "Steering for Safety, Hijacking for Profit: Trojan Threats in the LLM Activation Steering Supply Chain" (Yu Yan/Jiahao Chen/Siqi Lu/Yongjuan Wang/Ziming Zhao/Zhaoxuan Li, disjoint group): the first systematic security study of the STEERING-VECTOR SUPPLY CHAIN — the third-party plugin ecosystem that ships steering vectors to guide model behavior by intervening in internal representations. Audits EIGHT mainstream open-source steering-plugin libraries and finds three structural vulnerabilities (insufficient distribution-integrity verification, excessive plugin privileges, and unverified steering artifacts), then shows malicious plugins can disguise themselves as legitimate functionality and COVERTLY manipulate outputs without significantly degrading benign model performance — a trojaned steering vector/plugin as a supply-chain carrier. A new facet on 005's steering/activation-vector axis: prior 005 evidence treats the refusal/harmfulness direction as a mechanism to analyze or ablate; this weaponizes the DISTRIBUTION of steering interventions themselves (the activation-steering plugin supply chain), crosslinking adversarial-trigger-backdoor-004 (trojan carrier) and agentic-attack-surface-001 (plugin supply chain). Found via this run's cs.CR sweep. Rotated in via cap ROTATION over "Not All Refusals Are Equal" (2607.02714, 07-02, oldest evidence line — its domain-specific-abliteration-at-trillion-param-scale facet is carried forward by the frontier-scale single-direction MoE attack 2609.09793 (GLM-5.3-Flash 320B) and the open-weight-abliteration cross-links on 002/009; preserved in git/reports). last_evidence 10-10→10-26.
  - 2026-10-03 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04245 — "On the Steering Dimensionality of Refusal in Language Models": directly interrogates 005's founding single-"refusal-direction" assumption — additive steering should induce a behavior while directional ablation suppresses it, but behaviors may occupy richer activation GEOMETRIES than one direction, and semantically similar behaviors (safety-alignment refusal vs general-context refusal) may be carried by DISTINCT directions. Introduces STEERING DIMENSIONALITY — the minimum subspace dimensionality needed to reliably control a refusal behavior — and studies steering SUBSPACES rather than single vectors, generalizing the linear-direction account into a per-behavior multi-dimensional picture. The newer, more general refinement of the multi-dimensional-refusal-subspace facet (supersedes/de-dups the 07-02 RFM-AGOP line, which remains on study_shelf). Captured via this run's 005 targeted-axis dormancy probe (005 hit 21d on 10-20; the probe found this axis is ACTIVE, not silent → HELD, not dormant). Rotated in over "Fast Multi-dimensional Refusal Subspaces via RFM-AGOP" (2607.02396, 07-02, same facet, oldest, preserved on study_shelf). last_evidence 09-29→10-03.
  - 2026-09-09 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09793 — "How Fragile Is Safety Alignment at Frontier Scale? A Single-Direction Attack on a 320B MoE" (Shi/Chen/Shen, disjoint group): directional ablation — the canonical training-free single-"refusal-direction" white-box abliteration at 005's core, previously established only on DENSE models up to ~70B — SURVIVES the shift to a frontier mixture-of-experts model, GLM-5.3-Flash (320B, 288 routed experts, four-wide hyper-connection residual, block-FP8 quantized). But the refusal direction is no longer where the dense recipe looks: editing the attention / dense / routed-expert writers on their own removes almost none of the refusal behavior; only ablating across the DISTRIBUTED hyper-connected residual writers strips it. Extends the linear-refusal-direction account to frontier-scale MoE architecture + quantization and relocates the removal primitive — open-weight MoE scale does not buy alignment robustness. Found via this run's cs.CR sweep. Rotated in over "Has This Checkpoint Been Abliterated?" (2607.01854, 07-02 — a pre-deployment abliteration audit amply covered by the AMS activation-analysis scanner 08-06); resets last_evidence 08-26→09-09.
  - 2026-08-26 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25390 — "Refusal geometry reflects refusal training: diverse refusal prefixes can raise stable rank and weaken refusal vector ablation attacks" (Andrey Labunets, disjoint author): a case study on OLMo-2-0425-1B-Instruct showing the refusal DIRECTION/subspace is a direct imprint of the refusal-completion first-token training losses — explaining WHY safety-critical features concentrate into low-dimensional structure — and that this structure is BRITTLE: training on diverse refusal prefixes RAISES the activation stable rank and measurably WEAKENS refusal-vector-ablation (abliteration) attacks, the exact removal primitive at 005's core. A fresh mechanistic result tying refusal-direction geometry to the training procedure and turning that geometry into a defensive lever against ablation; found via this run's cs.CR sweep. Rotated in over the founding Robust-Harmful-Features line (2606.28153, 06-26 — its attack↔defense-duality point is amply covered by HARC response-side + the prefill study); resets last_evidence 08-06→08-26 (moots the 21-day dormancy check).
  - 2026-08-06 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05578 — "Detecting Safety Training Modification in Language Models via Activation Analysis" (AMS, Activation-based Model Scanner): detects modifications to safety training by measuring the GEOMETRIC STRUCTURE of safety-relevant concepts in activation space — safety training creates measurable separation between harmful/benign classes, and certain modifications (abliteration, uncensored fine-tunes) collapse or rotate that structure; validated across 14 model configs / 4 architecture families (Llama, Gemma, Qwen, Mistral) / 4 safety-modification categories (LOO-CV threshold accuracy 71%). A 10th independent group extending the linear-refusal/activation-geometry account to a deployable pre-deployment scanner for safety-training tampering — the abliteration-detection lineage of "Has This Checkpoint Been Abliterated?" (2607.01854) generalized to a geometric-collapse signal. Found via this session's targeted 005 dormancy check (see notes). Fresh 08-06 item — resets last_evidence 07-14→08-06.
  - 2026-07-09 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08883 — "Optimizing Against Safety Representations: Activation-Guided Adversarial Suffixes and the Geometry of Refusal": introduces Activation-Guided GCG, replacing GCG's output-based objective with a loss that DIRECTLY targets the model's internal refusal DIRECTION, plus Soft-GCG (a continuous relaxation) — finding that suppressing refusal GLOBALLY across all layers/positions beats targeting any single layer-position site, i.e. safety representations are distributed across the forward pass rather than causally localized. This is the pre-registered outstanding trigger the trend had watched since seeding — a genuine end-to-end steering/ACTIVATION-VECTOR JAILBREAK TOOL that operationalizes the refusal-direction account into an optimization attack (not another analysis/interpretability result); an 11th independent group. Rotated in this session; rotated out the founding "Geometry of Refusal" line (2606.22686, 06-21, oldest — preserved on study_shelf and as the trend's conceptual origin in these notes). Found via this session's targeted 005 dormancy check.
  - 2026-09-29 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.37737 — "Where Do LLMs Decide to Break the Rules? Mechanistic Localization of Prompt Injection Compliance": layer-by-layer causal activation patching across five models (4B-32B) shows adversarial-instruction (prompt-injection) COMPLIANCE is decided at a LATE-LAYER BOTTLENECK in the final third of the network — attack information is linearly decodable from layer 1 but has negligible causal leverage until that bottleneck, where patching reverses compliance in 77-92% of cases. The compliance mechanism occupies a COMPACT LINEAR SUBSPACE (rank-8 in 4B/14B models, scaling to rank-64 at 32B), architecturally stable across model families, and its causal-peak layer is also the representationally optimal site for detecting attacks (beating early-layer classifiers that degrade under leetspeak/surface obfuscation). Extends 005's linear refusal/harmfulness-direction account from harmful-content refusal to injection-COMPLIANCE — the same compact-linear-subspace + causal-bottleneck mechanism, now for the comply/refuse decision under prompt injection. Found via this run's cs.CR sweep. Rotated in via cap ROTATION over HARC (2607.00572, 07-01, oldest — its harmfulness/refusal direction-suppression facet is amply carried by the Internal-Attribution-Graphs 07-08 + Prefill 07-14 lines; preserved in git/reports). last_evidence 09-09→09-29.
  - 2026-10-10 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15218 — "Running Fewer Loops Removes Refusal Behaviour in Recurrent-Depth Language Models" (Azizi/Potraghloo/Fayyazi/Shokoufa/Pedram, disjoint): refusal behaviour in recurrent-depth (looped-computation) LMs DEPENDS on how many recurrent loops run at inference — running fewer loops removes refusal (~5.3x more harmful compliance) while the harm is still linearly decodable from internal state, so refusal is a shallow, compute-depth-dependent computation rather than a robust property. A novel ARCHITECTURAL-manipulation facet of the refusal-as-manipulable-mechanism axis (distinct from abliteration / steering-vector / prefill), captured this session from the 10-20 Proposal-N probe's rotate-candidate set. Rotated in over the oldest-redundant mechanistic-analysis line "Internal Attribution Graphs" (2607.07903, 07-08 — its graph-level causal-circuit diagnosis is carried by the Prefill 07-14 + PI-compliance-localization 09-29 lines; preserved in git/reports). last_evidence 10-03->10-10.
  - 2026-07-14 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14147 — "Breaking Refusal in the First Half: A Mechanistic Study of the Prefill Jailbreak": dissects the one-line prefill attack ("Sure, here is") that strips refusal — the harm REPRESENTATION stays intact (a linear probe reads harm at 0.91-0.98 on the flipped-to-compliant prompts, as high as on refused ones) while behavioral refusal drops to chance across four models / three families (1.5-3.8B and 14B), so refusal is a SHALLOW, response-site computation. Localizes it to an EARLY response window (a dose-matched position control shows the first half of the response suffices to break refusal, the second half is nearly inert), confirmed by three converging causal probes: restoring the harm direction there partially re-engages refusal, and injecting the model's own refuse-state reverses the jailbreak (74% held-out). 8th independent group on this trend's mechanistic axis — extends the linear-direction/suppression account (HARC, Robust-Harmful-Features, Internal-Attribution-Graphs) to the specific prefill-jailbreak mechanism and pins WHERE (early response positions) and HOW (harm intact, refusal computation shallow) it fails.
  - 2026-07-11 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.10112 — "Minionese: Comprehensive Benchmark and Mechanistic Study of Multilingual LLM Safety": an 18-language multilingual jailbreak benchmark (4 resource tiers, 4 perturbation types) paired with a GEOMETRIC MECHANISTIC analysis showing low-resource-language jailbreaks succeed by routing harmful content through a geometrically MISALIGNED refusal subspace, with a sharp Tier-2→3 safety-regime transition. 9th independent group on the mechanistic axis — extends the multi-dimensional refusal-subspace account (RFM-AGOP) to the MULTILINGUAL dimension, showing the same geometric-suppression mechanism explains cross-lingual jailbreak susceptibility, not just English adversarial prompting.

### [id: model-extraction-fingerprinting-006] Model extraction, capability distillation & fingerprinting under restrictive APIs
alias: model-extraction, distillation-attacks, model-fingerprinting
stage: emerging
confidence: medium
first_observed: 2026-07-04
last_evidence: 2026-10-24
evidence:
  - 2026-10-07 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.10909 — "Power Side-Channel Membership Inference Attack on Embedded Machine Learning" (PSCMIA): membership inference WITHOUT model outputs — existing MIAs need prediction probabilities or at least predicted labels, an assumption that breaks for on-device ML with suppressed/inaccessible outputs, but the data-dependent computations that produce those outputs stay observable through physical side channels. PSCMIA infers training-set membership directly from POWER TRACES (no probabilities, no labels), reaching ROC-AUC up to 0.907 on FC models and outperforming label-only MIA in 11/16 model-dataset-hardware configs (MNIST/FMNIST/CIFAR10/CINIC10; FC+CNN; STM32F3/XMEGA). Extends 006's membership-inference facet to the HARDWARE side-channel setting where conventional output-based MIA is defeated by output suppression. Rotated in via cap ROTATION (006 21-day dormancy re-check this run found fresh in-window on-axis work → trend HELD, not dormant); rotated OUT the synthetic-face dataset-MIA line (2607.29144, 07-25, oldest MIA facet, preserved in git/reports). last_evidence 09-18→10-07.
  - 2026-09-07 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07131 — "AgentLeak: Cloning Stronger LLM Agent Capabilities onto Weaker Agents Beyond Skill Stealing" (Lyu/Wu/Han et al., disjoint author set): a NEW extraction TARGET beyond skill-stealing — a substantially weaker attacker-controlled agent acquires a stronger proprietary agent's task-solving capability via limited black-box interaction, because recovering explicit skill artifacts does NOT transfer the implicit PROCEDURAL behaviors the stronger agent realizes; the skill-EXECUTION GAP (observable differences between successful victim runs and failed attacker runs) is itself the leakage surface. Distinct from 006's model-weights/architecture/embedding/harness/fingerprint targets (the extractable asset is procedural agent capability, not a skill file or model params) — a fresh independent group. Rotated in via cap ROTATION over Cross-Modal MIA (2607.04339, 07-05), refreshing 006 last_evidence 08-20→09-07 one day short of the 21-day dormancy re-check.
  - 2026-08-20 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.20055 — "EchoCoT: Extracting Hidden Chain-of-Thought from Large Reasoning Models": black-box near-verbatim extraction of concealed CoT from frontier LRMs via a previously-overlooked reasoning-REPLAY surface BETWEEN tool calls — iteratively extracting using API-returned fidelity signals, with an LLM-optimized universal injection trajectory that generalizes across unseen datasets; up to 66.4% near-verbatim on open-source LRMs (≥90% tokens exact, length within 10% of target), up to 80% on unseen data, and pulls a 33,463-token trace from a 32,948-token Gemini-2.5 target. The 2nd independent group establishing hidden-CoT as an extractable, fragile IP boundary — rotated in, dropping "Stealing Reasoning Traces from Proprietary LLM APIs" (2608.09867, 08-10, same CoT-extraction facet preserved on study_shelf). chain-of-thought by returning it to the client as ENCRYPTED blocks passed back with each subsequent request; these encrypted blocks are fully interchangeable across sessions, users and models within a provider's ecosystem, and injecting a foreign encrypted reasoning trace yields a scalable DECRYPTION jailbreak that recovers the concealed reasoning. An 11th independent group and a genuinely NEW extraction TARGET (the provider-concealed CoT/reasoning trace itself) plus CHANNEL (cross-session encrypted-block reuse), distinct from 006's weights/architecture/embedding/timing/harness/prompt targets. Rotated in this session; rotated out "Embedding Inference Attack" (2607.01276, 07-01) — the oldest/narrowest line, a black-box which-embedding-model fingerprinting precursor now superseded by One-Token's broader fingerprinting.
  - 2026-07-31 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29378 — "PTP: Previous-Token Prediction based LLM Inversion for Near-Exact Prompt Reconstruction": a NEW LLM-inversion technique reconstructing the ORIGINAL PROMPT near-exactly from observed model outputs — unlike prior LLM-inversion work, it does not require fine-tuning a sequence-to-sequence model on large external datasets nor access to the target's weights or logits, working from black-box generations alone. A genuinely distinct extraction TARGET (the input prompt itself, not weights/architecture/embeddings/harness) and technique (previous-token-prediction based reconstruction) from every other 006 evidence item — an 8th independent group on the extraction/fingerprinting core. Found via today's cs.LG explore-supplement, clean append (006 under cap at 7/10).
  - 2026-07-31 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28884 — "Hollow-LLM Attack: Computationally Trivial Weights in Zero-Knowledge Verification of LLM Inference": shows that zero-knowledge (ZK) LLM-inference verification — meant to prove a remote provider actually served the advertised model/weights rather than a tampered or downsized variant — can be DEFEATED with computationally-trivial "hollow" weights that still satisfy the ZK proof's equational-correctness check. A direct attack on the opaque-serving-chain TRUST layer 006 already tracks via One-Token fingerprinting and IRIS's substitution/dilution auditing, but from the opposite direction (breaking a cryptographic verification guarantee rather than statistically detecting a swap) — a 9th independent group, clean append.
  - 2026-07-30 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28147 — "Agent Harness Distillation": formalizes INFERENCE-TIME HARNESS EXTRACTION as a new IP-theft target in autonomous multi-agent systems (AMAS, e.g. Hermes) — unlike prior static multi-agent-system IP leakage, an AMAS's coordination harness (the iteratively-optimized reasoning/action orchestration co-evolved with the underlying LLM) emerges dynamically at inference time, and this paper shows it can be extracted/exploited via black-box interaction alone. A 7th independent group and a genuinely NEW extraction TARGET (the agent harness itself, distinct from 006's existing model-weights/architecture/embedding/fingerprint targets) — found via today's cs.CR sweep, clean append (006 was under cap at 6/10).
  - 2026-02-23 — https://www.anthropic.com/news/detecting-and-preventing-distillation-attacks — Anthropic: three industrial-scale in-the-wild distillation campaigns (DeepSeek 150k+, Moonshot 3.4M+, MiniMax 13M+ exchanges) illicitly extracting Claude's reasoning/tool-use/coding capabilities through ~24,000 fraudulent accounts and "hydra cluster" proxy networks; attributed with high confidence via IP/metadata/infra correlation. First vendor-published in-the-wild confirmation of large-scale frontier-model capability extraction via distillation, with concrete detection/response countermeasures.
  - 2026-09-13 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.14379 — "Cryptanalytic Extraction of Neural Networks Without Known Architecture Assumption" (Shen/Chen/Wang/Yu/Wang): removes the standing assumption behind ALL prior cryptanalytic extraction — that the attacker knows the network architecture — with a guess-and-determine framework that jointly recovers ReLU fully-connected depth + per-layer dimensions AND parameters from black-box outputs, keyed on architecture-sensitive traces (a "zero suffix" in merged weight vectors) left by dimension guessing (attack; extraction-core advance, supersedes NightVision's single-logit architecture-recovery line).
  - 2026-09-18 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21941 — "End-to-End Hard-Label Cryptanalytic Model Extraction Using Efficient Sign Recovery": completes black-box cryptanalytic extraction in the HARDEST oracle setting — where the adversary observes only the final output LABEL (e.g. "dog"/"cat"), not logits — by making practical the sign-recovery step that Carlini et al.'s Eurocrypt-2025 polynomial-time hard-label ReLU-MLP extraction left query- and compute-prohibitive, yielding a full end-to-end hard-label parameter-extraction pipeline. Extends 006's cryptanalytic-extraction core (pairs with Cryptanalytic-Extraction-Without-Architecture 2609.14379, 09-13) into the label-only threat model — a distinct, strictly harder setting. Rotated in via cap ROTATION; rotated OUT One-Token (2607.10252, 07-11, oldest by date) — its single-token fingerprinting / opaque-serving-chain-deviation facet preserved in git/reports (re-queue if the aggregator-fingerprinting facet needs a fresh anchor). last_evidence 09-13→09-18.
  - 2026-10-24 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33671 — "Extraction meets Distillation: cryptanalytic techniques to recover parameters from a Deep Neural Network": combines cryptanalytic weight-DIRECTION extraction with learning from predicted LABELS to reconstruct fully-connected networks — adapts a ReLU extraction implementation to LEAKY ReLU, compares sign-search methods, estimates biases and output layers, and proves conditional bounds for direction recovery (approximate prefixes, local-distance-vote preservation, aligned-parameter-error prediction changes, independent-scoring sign selection) plus exact row restoration distinguished from gradient masking. A cryptanalytic-extraction core advance on 006 (pairs with the architecture-free 2609.14379 and hard-label 2609.21941 lines), with the honest caveat that the evaluated setting is ASSISTED extraction (privileged access to gradients/hidden activations) rather than the strict black-box threat model of the other cryptanalytic lines. Found IN-WINDOW via the 21-day dormancy targeted-axis check → 006 HELD (capture, not dormancy). Cap-ROTATION in (006 at cap); rotated OUT LeakyLMs (2607.20723, 07-22, oldest by date) — its per-token-timing side-channel extraction facet preserved in git/reports (re-queue if the timing-channel facet needs a fresh anchor). last_evidence 10-07→10-24.

### [id: embodied-physical-injection-007] Physical / perception-channel prompt injection against embodied & wearable AI agents
alias: physical-prompt-injection, embodied-agent-attacks, sensory-channel-injection, kinetic-prompt-injection
stage: dormant
confidence: medium
first_observed: 2026-07-14
last_evidence: 2026-10-07
evidence:
  - 2026-10-07 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09708 — "Black-Box Adversarial Patch Attacks on VLAs via Ancestor VLM Exploitation": most Vision-Language-Action robot models (VLAs) are adapted from a publicly-released pretrained VLM and inherit its visual perception + instruction-conditioned grounding, so an adversary can attack a DEPLOYED VLA using ONLY its ANCESTOR VLM — no white-box access to the VLA and no surrogate VLA. Three adversarial-PATCH attacks placed in the robot's visual field disrupt the inherited capabilities: a vision-disruption attack corrupting projected visual tokens (relative + absolute), an instruction-grounded semantic-evidence subversion, and a combined patch. A black-box perception-channel attack on embodied VLA agents under a realistic deployment threat model, and a fresh independent group distinct from 007's scene-text (RIPA/Devil-in-the-Lens), audio (Piggybacking), tactile (GhostTac/EMI) and sensor-telemetry (Seeing-is-Not-Believing) cases — the first adversarial-patch/perturbation facet (vs instruction/scene-text injection) on the nucleus. Clean append (9→10, now at cap); last_evidence 09-25→10-07, deepening the dominant LLM/VLM-controlled-robot device class (cross-device-class-transfer caveat (c) still open).

### [id: hallucination-squatting-008] Weaponized LLM hallucination: predictable resource-name hallucination pre-registered as an AI supply-chain attack (slopsquatting)
alias: slopsquatting, hallucination-squatting, phantom-squatting, package-hallucination-attacks
stage: dormant
confidence: medium
first_observed: 2026-07-15
last_evidence: 2026-10-07
evidence:
  - 2026-10-07 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09264 — "Package Hallucination Attacks on Coding Agents through Prompt Injection in Rule Files" (PackHallu): agentic coding frameworks increasingly load community-shared RULE FILES (e.g. `.cursorrules`) to guide autonomous code generation; PackHallu injects malicious prompts into benign rule files to induce the coding agent to replace legitimate dependencies with attacker-controlled packages, using an evolutionary optimization loop that rewrites the injected prompts from trajectory-level feedback + LLM-guided mutations. High attack success and strong transferability across multiple benchmarks, LLMs and agent frameworks. The FIRST fresh OFFENSIVE primary on the package-hallucination/slopsquatting axis since this trend went dormant — a research attack (not defense) that weaponizes package-name hallucination through a new delivery channel (PI in shared rule files), a crossover with agentic-attack-surface-001's rule-file/PI surface. REVIVES 008 (dormant→seed) and meets the pre-registered offensive-revive trigger (a fresh cross-model offensive attack/measurement on the package-hallucination axis) that the W33→W40 dormancy checks found unmet → CANCELS the W41 (~10-10) archive. last_evidence 07-14→10-07.
  - 2026-07-01 — https://unit42.paloaltonetworks.com/phantom-squatting-hallucinated-web-domains/ — Unit 42 "Phantom Squatting": in-the-wild vendor confirmation that LLMs consistently hallucinate web DOMAINS for legitimate brands and adversaries are ACTIVELY registering these nonexistent domains to intercept AI-generated traffic — slopsquatting at the web-domain level, quantifying a live attack surface (the deployed-in-the-wild datapoint of this cluster).
  - 2026-07-08 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.07433 — "Beware of Agentic Botnets" (adversarial HalluSquatting): exploits LLMs' tendency to hallucinate RESOURCE IDENTIFIERS (repos, skills) to mount scalable UNTARGETED promptware attacks with no direct injection channel — the attacker computes the LLM's hallucination distribution over resource names and PRE-REGISTERS the hallucinated names to host adversarial prompts; hallucination reaches up to 85% (repo cloning) / 100% (skill installation), transfers across foundation models, and yields remote tool/code execution and an agentic BOTNET against production LLM apps with integrated terminals.
  - 2026-07-14 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.12340 — "Skills That Don't Exist": the first LARGE-SCALE measurement of skill-name hallucination in LLM agents (15,000 prompts across 12 configurations) — agents frequently invent skill names that exist in no registry; because registries rarely verify publishers, an adversary can harvest the fake names an agent returns, pre-register malicious skills under them, and wait for a victim to install the payload. Quantifies the hallucinated-skill supply-chain attack surface, independent of the botnet-framed HalluSquatting group and Unit 42's in-the-wild domain result.
  - 2026-05-16 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17062 — Aleksandr Churilov "The Range Shrinks, the Threat Remains: Re-evaluating LLM Package Hallucinations on the 2026 Frontier-Model Cohort" (v2 revised 2026-06-11; coordinated-disclosure outcome reported by Socket.dev 2026-07-22): an independent replication of the Spracklen et al. USENIX Security '25 methodology on five frontier code models released Oct-2025→Mar-2026 (Claude Sonnet 4.6, Claude Haiku 4.5, GPT-5.4-mini, Gemini 2.5 Pro, DeepSeek V3.2) across 199,845 paired Python/JavaScript prompts validated against PyPI and npm master lists. Hallucination rates compressed by an order of magnitude in SPREAD (4.62%-6.10%, vs Spracklen's 5.2%-21.7%) but did not disappear — and the security result is the convergence, not the rate: 127 package names (109 PyPI, 18 npm) are invented IDENTICALLY by ALL FIVE models, and after coordinated disclosure with PyPI Security and Socket.dev, 53 of them (41 PyPI, 12 npm) remain registrable past each registry's existing defenses. A MODEL-AGNOSTIC pre-registration target set: one malicious registration reaches the users of every major model provider at once, which no prior 008 evidence establishes (Unit 42 measured domains in the wild; HalluSquatting and Skills-That-Don't-Exist each measured a single-model hallucination distribution). A 4th independent group. Found via today's targeted 008 dormancy check.
  - 2026-07-05 — https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21199427 — Daniel Okwor (Orygn LLC) "From Hallucination to Registration: Do the Package Names That LLMs Invent Actually Get Claimed?": the first COUNTER-RESULT on this trend's axis, and the first study to measure the step that decides whether anyone is actually harmed. Five open-weight code models over 6,800 generations yield 1,641 distinct package names confirmed absent from npm/PyPI; the supply does not saturate (a second 2,000-generation sweep still added 455 unseen names at nearly the original rate, so the invented-name space is effectively endless). But following ALL 1,641 to the registries, every single one remains UNREGISTERED — claimed by neither attacker nor defender. Turning to 149 hallucinated names from published datasets that have circulated publicly long enough to attract attention, 22 are registered and, on reading their contents, none are attacks. Conclusion: hallucination is EXPOSURE, not realized harm — the measured attack surface is real and unbounded while in-the-wild exploitation of it remains essentially absent. A 5th independent group, and material calibration evidence AGAINST escalating this trend on hallucination-rate measurements alone. Found via today's targeted 008 dormancy check.
  - 2026-05-28 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.29354 — "Harmless Yet Harmful: Neutral Prompting Attacks for Stealthy Hallucination Steering in Agent Skills" (NPA): the first ATTACK that AMPLIFIES the slopsquatting surface rather than measuring natural hallucination — semantically benign instructions (e.g. encouraging imagination/exhaustiveness) increase an LLM coding agent's package-hallucination propensity WITHOUT any explicit malicious intent and, unlike targeted dependency steering, WITHOUT specifying an attacker-chosen name (so it evades intent-based detection). Turns the attacker's job from "wait for a useful hallucination" into "provoke more hallucinations to pre-register", a distinct offensive lever on this trend's mechanism. A 6th independent group. Found via this session's targeted 008 dormancy check (W32).
  - 2026-06-07 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08444 — "When LLMs Invent Rust Crates: An Empirical Study of Hallucination Patterns and Mitigation": the first large-scale study of crate (package) hallucination in LLM-generated RUST code, extending the slopsquatting measurement beyond the Python/JavaScript ecosystems prior work covered; a multi-source dataset (Stack Overflow / GitHub / LLM-generated tasks) over commercial + open models under varied decoding finds Rust hallucination follows a DISTINCT pattern — different models hallucinate surprisingly CONSISTENT crate names (a cross-model convergence that, as in Churilov's Python/JS result, makes a single malicious registration reach many models' users). A 7th independent group; a new-ecosystem measurement facet. Found via this session's targeted 008 dormancy check (W32).
