### [id: self-evolving-agent-poisoning-010] Poisoning the experience→skill promotion pipeline of self-evolving agents: untrusted experience laundered into trusted, persistent skills
alias: self-evolving-agent-attacks, skill-pipeline-poisoning, trajectory-poisoning, experience-to-skill-laundering, persistent-carrier-poisoning
stage: dormant
confidence: medium
first_observed: 2026-08-07
last_evidence: 2026-10-06
evidence:
  - 2026-10-06 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07645 — "SkillPoison: Progressive Skill Poisoning via Successful Experiences" (disjoint author set): skill poisoning arising from VERIFIED SUCCESSFUL experiences with NO individually-malicious trajectory — SkillPoison first constructs successful experiences that reinforce a target behavior, then REMOVES the contextual conditions that constrain WHEN the behavior applies, shaping how the skill EXTRACTOR GENERALIZES so useful behavior supports task success while harmful behavior is induced on misapplication; 95.71% ASR across three benchmarks while every injected experience stays task-correct and passes verification + lexical inspection (code released). 9th independent group on this trend's runtime-trust-inversion pipeline, attacking the extractor's GENERALIZATION step — distinct from the distillation (SkillJack), promotion/attribution (PoisonedEvolution), skill-generation (EvoSkill) and self-eval-benchmark (Reflections-on-Trusting-Trust) control points already on evidence, and sharpening EvoBreak's benign-composition idea into generalization-shaping. Clean APPEND (8→9, under cap). Found via this run's cs.CR sweep; last_evidence 09-15→10-06 (moots the 21-day dormancy line — 010 was at 22d).
  - 2026-09-15 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17817 — "Reflections on Trusting Trust, Revisited: Contaminating Self-Modifying AI Coding Agents with Poisoned Benchmarks" (disjoint author set): recasts Thompson's compiler-Trojan attack for self-modifying coding agents — an adversary who supplies POISONED BENCHMARKS to the agent's self-evaluation/self-improvement process induces FUTURE versions of the agent to write vulnerable code on clean, held-out tasks. Instantiated end-to-end against three recently-proposed self-modifying agents (Darwin Gödel Machine with experimental modifications, the Self-Improving Coding Agent, and Hyperagents — the latter two substantively unmodified): e.g. Hyperagents powered by Sonnet 4.5 self-evolves instructions that DISABLE HTTPS certificate validation on neutral URL-fetching tasks, and the contamination PERSISTS even when the poisoned agent is subsequently evolved against clean benchmarks. 8th independent group on this trend's runtime-trust-inversion pipeline, attacking a NEW control point — the self-evaluation/self-improvement benchmark loop — distinct from the experience-distillation (SkillJack), promotion/attribution (PoisonedEvolution) and skill-generation (EvoSkill) points already on evidence. Also study_shelf.
  - 2026-08-31 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.30429 — "EvoSkill Injection: Red-Teaming Autonomous Skill Generation and Evolution in Self-Evolving Agents" (Doyun Kim/Chanwoo Kim/Sugyeong Eo/Yeo-Chan Yoon/Chanjun Park, disjoint author set): defines EvoSkill Injection — a threat model targeting the autonomous skill GENERATION/evolution pipeline of self-evolving agents where malicious capabilities are generated, stored and reused as legitimate skills — and ships SARGE, a red-teaming framework that exercises it. 7th independent group on this trend's runtime-trust-inversion pipeline; surfaced via the 010 dormancy targeted-axis check (a capture the 09-01 sweep missed), dated inside the recorded-silent window.
  - 2026-08-26 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25776 — "EVOMAL: Self-Poisoning in Self-Evolving Coding Agents" (Xiaodong Wu/Yu Shi/Qi Li/Zhimin Zhao/Xiangman Li/Bram Adams, disjoint author set): self-evolving LLM coding agents author their own tools by imitating retrieved skills; a retrieved MALICIOUS skill becomes the template for a NEW skill that preserves the payload — the agent authors, stores and runs the resulting malicious skill (self-poisoning). EvoMal amplifies it by wrapping an interchangeable payload in a "banner" of benign-looking structural elements that induces the imitating agent to reproduce the enclosed code; the attacker only PLANTS skills (never invokes them), and each authored copy re-enters the library to be imitated again — a self-propagating WORM that PERSISTS after the planted skills are removed. 6th independent group on this trend's runtime-trust-inversion pipeline, and the first to close it into a self-propagating persistent worm (past SkillJack's single-skill post-deletion persistence).
  - 2026-08-07 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06862 — "SynChain: Inducing Computer-Use Agent Systems to Construct Their Own Attack Chains" (Fuyao Zhang/Jiaming Zhang/Che Wang, disjoint author set): computer-use agents that generate/store/reuse artifacts (skills, memory entries) can have malicious influence covertly embedded into the STRUCTURAL REDUNDANCIES of their autonomously-synthesized artifacts, so compromise propagates INTERNALLY through the agent's own persistent state — surviving internal state updates and bypassing standard vetting. A self-synthesized attack that closes the loop EvoBreak/SkillJack/PoisonedEvolution opened: not just poisoning an input experience but the agent laundering the malice into its own future-reusable artifacts. 4th independent group on the runtime-trust-inversion pipeline.
  - 2026-08-07 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06984 — "HarnessSafe: Evaluating Safety Across Persistent Carriers in Agent Harnesses" (Xiao Zhang/Yusheng Wang/Yuhao Fei, disjoint author set): the first cross-harness BENCHMARK for this trend's failure mode — 328 executable cases across SEVEN persistent-carrier families (memory, skills, tools, shared artifacts, …) on mainstream agent harnesses, each specified as a Persistent-Risk Lifecycle tracing attacker-influenced content crossing system boundaries to later affect a benign request. Moves the sub-theme past single-attack demos to systematic measurement of how persistent-state risk propagates. 5th independent group; supplies the measurement side.
  - 2026-08-06 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05563 — "When Experience Becomes Instruction: Trajectory Poisoning in Self-Evolving Agent Skill Systems" (PoisonedEvolution, Chen/Jiang/Deng/Du): a trajectory-poisoning attack on the PROMOTION process of self-evolving skill (SES) systems that distill agent trajectories into persistent skills — "allowing untrusted experience to become trusted instruction". A skill-visible black-box attacker (can inspect a target skill and contribute bounded evidence, but cannot observe private pools/evolution logic or edit the skill bank) achieves poisoning via three requirements — Inclusion, Evolution Attribution and Realization — with Attribution the distinctive bottleneck: the target behavior must appear causally useful, recurrent and generalizable before the system will promote it. Evaluated across four representative security-effect families with inert canaries. Isolates the promotion/attribution step as the attack-critical control point, distinct from SkillJack's distillation-whitewashing and EvoBreak's benign-composition mechanism.
  - 2026-08-05 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03509 — "SkillJack: Persistent Skill Backdoors in Self-Evolving Agents" (Ying/Wu/Wu/Zheng; code on Tencent AI-Infra-Guard): the first attack on the experience→skill pipeline where poisoned experiences are transformed BY THE AGENT ITSELF into durable malicious skills — sanitization-whitewashing drops the extracted skill's safety-detection rate 98.5%→11.4%, 56–89% ASR across SkillX/Anything2Skill, 80% of implanted skills PERSIST after the original poisoned records are deleted, and some fire even on benign queries. A provenance/skill-lifecycle attack surface distinct from memory/retrieval poisoning: deleting the poisoned source record is not enough because the malice has already been laundered into a standalone skill. Also on study_shelf.
  - 2026-08-04 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01759 — "EvoBreak" (Yan/Zhang/Li/Zhou; ships BreakGym): an experience-conditioned sequential attack on self-evolving agents in which only the COMPOSITION of individually-benign experiences crosses the safety boundary — no single contributed experience is itself malicious, so per-item content inspection cannot catch it; the safety failure is a property of how experiences aggregate over the evolution trajectory. Establishes that the compositional/temporal structure of the experience stream, not any one poisoned item, is the exploitable surface.
notes: SEEDED 2026-08-07 on the pre-registered convergence trigger the daily/weekly notes tracked as the "SELF-EVOLVING-AGENT attack nucleus" since 08-05 (EvoBreak 08-04 + SkillJack 08-05 = 2 groups, "watch for a 3rd"). The 3rd independent group landed via today's cs.CR sweep — PoisonedEvolution (2608.05563) — clearing the ≥3-independent-group + ≥1-artifact seed bar. Mechanism (scope axis 1): self-evolving agents distill their own trajectories/experiences into a persistent SKILL bank, inverting the trust boundary — UNTRUSTED experience is promoted to TRUSTED reusable instruction and survives deletion of the source record. Distinct from 001 (attacks on an already-trusted STATIC stack — memory/RAG/MCP/skills-as-files) and from 004 (training/weight backdoors): here the attack is on the runtime learning loop that MINTS new trusted capability, with persistence in the skill bank not the weights. The groups attack distinct control points of the SAME pipeline — composition (EvoBreak), distillation (SkillJack), promotion/attribution (PoisonedEvolution), skill-generation (EvoSkill), self-eval-benchmark (Reflections), and now generalization-shaping (SkillPoison). Full seed/author-independence narrative in git/reports. Standing confidence caveat: all three are academic attack papers from the same ~2-week window with no in-the-wild case and no independent defense yet, and "self-evolving skill system" spans several concrete architectures (SkillX, Anything2Skill, the SES abstraction) — confidence medium until (a) a 4th independent group OR a defense group lands, (b) an in-the-wild self-evolving-agent deployment is shown poisoned this way, or (c) the mechanism is shown to transfer across skill-system architectures rather than being per-benchmark. Watch also for whether the broader "agent learns at runtime" surface (memory consolidation, RAG-write-back) converges with the skill-promotion surface into one runtime-trust-boundary trend. PROMOTED seed→emerging 2026-08-10 (daily): two fresh independent groups landed in the 08-07 cs.CR batch — SynChain (2608.06862, self-synthesized attack chains propagating through the agent's own persistent state) and HarnessSafe (2608.06984, a 328-case cross-harness benchmark over 7 persistent-carrier families) — taking the cluster to FIVE disjoint author groups spanning both attack (EvoBreak/SkillJack/PoisonedEvolution/SynChain) and systematic-measurement (HarnessSafe) modalities: multi-source and forming, the emerging bar. Author-independence re-verified via arXiv metadata (SynChain = Fuyao Zhang/Jiaming Zhang/Che Wang; HarnessSafe = Xiao Zhang/Yusheng Wang/Yuhao Fei — both disjoint from each other and the prior three). Confidence held medium (still all-academic, same ~2-week window, no in-the-wild case; HarnessSafe now supplies the missing benchmark, one of the three caveat-lifting conditions). 2026-08-27 (daily): +1 evidence (clean APPEND, under cap) — EVOMAL (2608.25776, 08-26, disjoint author set), a 6th independent group that extends the pipeline from single-skill persistence to a SELF-PROPAGATING WORM: an imitation-authored malicious skill re-enters the library and persists after the planted seed is removed. last_evidence 08-07→08-26 — refreshes the trend from a 19-day quiet, well inside the 21-day dormancy window (no dormancy call was due). Stage HELD emerging (a 6th group in the same academic-attack modality is confirming, not yet the "broad, fast" accelerating bar — still all-academic, no in-the-wild case; but the worm/self-propagation escalation is noted as the next thing to watch for cross-architecture transfer). Confidence held medium. 2026-09-16 (daily): 010 hit the 21-day dormancy line (last_evidence 08-26, today 09-16). Per the mandated TARGETED-AXIS-CHECK BEFORE DORMANCY, ran arXiv all:-field queries over the silent window (`all:"self-evolving" AND (poisoning OR backdoor OR attack)`; `all:"self-evolving agent" AND cat:cs.CR`) → surfaced EvoSkill Injection / SARGE (2608.30429, 08-31, Kim et al.) UNCAPTURED and dated INSIDE the window = a capture failure → 010 HELD emerging (NOT dormant), EvoSkill appended as evidence (7th independent group), last_evidence 08-26→08-31 (moots the dormancy call). The same check surfaced Query-Only Backdoor Attacks on Self-Evolving Skills (2608.08303, 08-08, Luo/Wang/Shu) uncaptured but PRE-window → routed to observation_queue (capture-leak remediation), not evidence. Stage/confidence held medium (still all-academic, no in-the-wild case). 2026-09-17 (daily): +1 evidence (clean APPEND, under cap 7→8) — "Reflections on Trusting Trust, Revisited" (2609.17817, 09-15, disjoint author set): benchmark/self-evaluation poisoning of self-MODIFYING coding agents (Darwin Gödel Machine / Self-Improving Coding Agent / Hyperagents) — poisoned self-eval benchmarks induce future agent versions to write vulnerable code on clean held-out tasks (PoC: Hyperagents on Sonnet 4.5 self-evolves instructions disabling HTTPS cert validation), contamination PERSISTS after re-evolving on clean benchmarks. 8th independent group, attacking a NEW control point (the self-evaluation/self-improvement benchmark loop) distinct from the experience-distillation/promotion/skill-generation points; also study_shelf. last_evidence 08-31→09-15 (refreshes cadence from a 17-day quiet, well inside the 21-day dormancy window). Stage HELD emerging (8th group in the academic-attack modality — confirming, not the "broad, fast" accelerating bar; still no in-the-wild case). Confidence held medium. 2026-10-07 (daily): +1 CLEAN APPEND (8→9) — SkillPoison (2610.07645, 10-06, disjoint author set): a 9th independent group showing skill poisoning can arise from VERIFIED SUCCESSFUL experiences (no malicious trajectory) by shaping the skill EXTRACTOR's generalization — removing the contextual conditions that bound a behavior so it misapplies harmfully, 95.71% ASR with all injected experiences passing verification/lexical inspection. Attacks the extractor's generalization step, a new control point (distinct from distillation/promotion/skill-generation/self-eval lines). 010 had reached 22d quiet (past the 21-day line); SkillPoison moots the dormancy call. last_evidence 09-15→10-06. ### [id: automated-agent-redteam-011] Autonomous/agentic red-teaming systems that recon and attack OTHER production AI agents, building reusable attack knowledge
alias: automated-agent-red-teaming, agent-vs-agent-attack, autonomous-agent-pentest, ai-red-team-automation
stage: emerging
confidence: medium
first_observed: 2026-08-08
last_evidence: 2026-10-20
evidence:
  - 2026-10-20 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28966 — "Black-Box Red-Teaming of Multi-Agent Systems via Reinforcement Learning" (Yuqi Jia/Hangsheng Zhang/Chenxi Liu/Junjie Liang +1, disjoint group): an autonomous red-teamer that LEARNS an attack POLICY via reinforcement learning to compromise multi-agent LLM systems in a BLACK-BOX setting (no target internals) — a method-distinct 10th independent group on 011's autonomous-agent-attacker axis (RL-optimized attacks on MAS), distinct from the recon/PI attackers (KYA/SIR/SAGE-RT), the strategy/skill-evolving attackers (PIMiner/RedEvoAgent/ToolHazard), the white-box repo→runtime auditor (AgentXploit) and the persistent-state attacker (AutoRedClaw). Clean APPEND (9→10, now at cap); last_evidence 10-19→10-20.
  - 2026-10-19 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27244 — "AutoRedClaw: Automated Red-Teaming for Claw-Style Agents" (Hao Li/Tongyan Hu/Weidi Luo/Shanghao Shi/Xiaogeng Liu/Aiden Suh, disjoint author set): as agent systems shift from task-scoped tool-use agents to persistent personalized assistants (OpenClaw-style "claw agents" carrying config files such as AGENTS.md, long-term memory and reusable skills), that persistent state becomes a repeatedly-reloaded attack surface — but existing security-evaluation systems rely on manually-constructed test cases/environments that do not scale, go stale as agents evolve, and leave a gap with real deployment. AutoRedClaw is an automated red-teaming framework that AUTONOMOUSLY GENERATES end-to-end attacks TOGETHER WITH the environments needed to exercise them against claw-style agents, closing that scale/coverage gap. A 9th independent group on 011's autonomous-agent-attacker axis and the first to target the persistent-state (config/memory/skill) surface of personalized claw-style assistants at scale — distinct from the black-box recon/PI attackers (KYA/SIR/SAGE-RT), the strategy/skill-evolving attackers (PIMiner/RedEvoAgent/ToolHazard) and the white-box repo→runtime auditor (AgentXploit). REVIVES 011 (dormant→emerging): a fresh 011-axis group dated inside the archive window meets the pre-registered revive trigger (a 9th autonomous-agent-red-team group). Clean APPEND (8→9, under cap); last_evidence 09-25→10-19, archive-watch cleared.
  - 2026-09-25 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31318 — "AgentXploit: Autonomous Repository-to-Runtime Red-Teaming for AI Agents": an autonomous auditor for authorized WHITE-BOX pre-deployment auditing — the auditor has access to the target agent's repository and a controlled runtime, but successful attacks must still act through the task-defined attacker interface and be confirmed by an EXTERNAL VERIFIER (path-traversal / command-injection in the surrounding software, or adversarial content that redirects tool use). A two-role auditing design that recons the repo→runtime surface and manufactures verified attacks. An 8th independent group and the first to seat this trend's autonomous-attacker loop in a white-box, repo-grounded pre-deployment auditing setting (prior items were black-box recon/PI). Clean append (011 under cap); last_evidence 09-09→09-25, mooting the 09-30 dormancy line.
  - 2026-09-09 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09647 — "Black-Box Red Teaming of Agentic AI: A Taxonomy-Driven Framework for Automated Risk Discovery": a fully-automated black-box red-teaming framework (SAGE-RT) that, from only basic system descriptions, generates 120 adversarial scenarios per domain across a seven-domain risk taxonomy and runs human-validated LLM-judge evaluation against PRODUCTION agent architectures (CrewAI, AutoGen, four base models) — surfacing 56% average governance risk and 65% privacy risk in multi-agent configurations. A 7th independent group: an autonomous red-teamer that recons a target agent system and manufactures reusable, taxonomy-organized attack scenarios at scale (distinct from SIR's self-improving CUA injections and RedEvoAgent's skill-evolution). Captured via the Proposal-N pre-dormancy targeted-axis check (see notes) — an in-window capture miss, so 011 is HELD emerging rather than marked dormant.

### [id: llm-resource-exhaustion-dos-012] Economic/availability DoS on LLM systems: resource-amplification & cost-inflation attacks that preserve output correctness
alias: economic-dos, resource-amplification-attacks, inference-cost-attacks, llm-availability-attacks, sponge-attacks, energy-latency-attacks
stage: accelerating
confidence: medium
first_observed: 2026-08-13
last_evidence: 2026-10-22
evidence:
  - 2026-10-22 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30798 — "Amplifying Latency and Cost of LLM Inference via Rowhammer Attack" (LLVMhammer): the FIRST Rowhammer attack that degrades LLM inference performance while PRESERVING output correctness — a hardware-fault facet new to this axis. A single Rowhammer-induced bit-flip in the CPU DRAM holding Triton's MLIR/LLVM JIT compilation pipeline flips a performance-critical optimization pass, so Triton emits a functionally IDENTICAL but SUBOPTIMAL GPU kernel; because Triton caches the generated kernel on disk, one successful bit-flip causes PERSISTENT slowdown across all subsequent inference runs, and Triton's target-independent stages make the attack GPU-vendor-agnostic. Extends 012's output-correctness-preserving availability/cost thesis from the prompt/skill/gateway/serving layers DOWN to the inference compiler + hardware-fault layer — a distinct research community (memory-fault/microarchitecture) sustaining the axis. Cap-ROTATION in (012 at cap); rotated OUT the oldest line "From Role Prompt to Infinite Thinking" (2607.25936, 07-29). last_evidence 10-17→10-22.
  - 2026-10-17 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.24784 — "LLM Trojans that Increase Response Lengths and Cost": a data-poisoning Trojan planted in an instruction-tuned LLM that, when its trigger appears in the prompt, inflates the RESPONSE LENGTH (and hence the user's per-token cost) while otherwise behaving normally — and it fires not on an exact string but on SEMANTICALLY SIMILAR instructions, so the cost-inflation blends into ordinary use rather than producing the abnormally long/repetitive outputs that correctness-agnostic detectors can flag. A supply-chain / weights-resident facet of 012's cost-inflation axis (task behavior preserved → correctness-based detection stays structurally blind), distinct from the inference-time prompt (persona-conditioning), skill (SkillBloat/CDH), router (Load-Hijack), provider (PTIA), gateway (Cooldown-Landmines) and spec-decoding (Speedbumps) vectors already on evidence; crosslinks adversarial-trigger-backdoor-004 (the carrier is a trojan trigger). 9→10 CLEAN APPEND; last_evidence 10-07→10-17 (moots the ~10-28 dormancy line).
  - 2026-10-07 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.10929 — "Speedbumps: Rejection Attacks on Speculative Decoding" (SRAs): a NEW cost/availability attack surface in the draft-target interaction of speculative decoding — an attacker-appended adversarial suffix makes draft and target models DISAGREE more often, so fewer draft tokens are accepted per cycle and more target forward-passes are needed per generated token, inflating the victim's inference cost/latency (in some cases slower than plain autoregressive decoding). Two variants optimize expected accepted-prefix length from the target's proposal probability (Speedbump-P) or draft/target distribution overlap (Speedbump-D); suffixes survive sampling and TRANSFER across drafters (P) or across targets sharing a drafter (D). Output-correctness-preserving economic/availability DoS facet (cf FragToken token-amplification, Cooldown-Landmines cross-tenant) targeting an inference-SPEEDUP optimization itself. last_evidence 10-04→10-07.
  - 2026-10-04 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.05089 — "Cooldown Landmines: Cross-Tenant Interference Attacks on LLM Gateways": the 8th independent group and the first SHARED-STATE cross-tenant availability facet — LLM gateways enforce separate per-tenant quotas but SHARE model deployments and "cooldown" records that temporarily exclude failing backends, so one tenant's request failures mutate the shared cooldown state and restrict OTHER tenants' access to serviceable deployments. Two attacks on LiteLLM: (1) requests rejected at the key's RPM limit still reach failure handling, so caller-supplied identifiers for known registered deployments let two rejected requests redirect another tenant to fallback with zero upstream calls; (2) admitted traffic creates persistent cooldown records that degrade peers. A denial-of-availability vector that needs no amplification/cost-inflation — it weaponizes the gateway's own multi-tenant isolation gap — and lands on the SAME AI-gateway layer (LiteLLM) as 001's "LLM Heist", here for availability rather than key theft. Clean append (012 at 7→8, under cap); last_evidence 09-25→10-04.
  - 2026-09-25 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31552 — "FragToken: Amplifying LLM Inference Costs through Noncanonical Token Generation": a NEW token-level cost-amplification surface arising from the many-to-one mapping from token sequences to decoded text — standard LLMs emit the canonical token sequence, but an attacker can induce NONCANONICAL token sequences that decode to the same (or benign) text while consuming far more tokens, so cost inflation blends into normal-looking traffic instead of the abnormally long/repetitive outputs prior attacks produce (which are easier to detect and dilute when benign traffic dominates). 7th independent group; the trend's defining property holds (output correctness/utility preserved → correctness-based detection stays structurally blind) with a stealthier, token-level vector. Clean append (012 under cap); last_evidence 09-17→09-25.
  - 2026-09-17 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20370 — "The More It Says, the More You Pay: A Black-Box Audit of Provider-Side Token Inflation in LLM Services" (PTIA): the FIRST provider-as-attacker facet on this axis — in pay-per-token services a DISHONEST PROVIDER covertly manipulates generation to inflate output tokens while largely preserving task utility (five attacks instantiated at the query/prompt/representation/model levels of the provider-controlled pipeline, each raising mean output length to >10.2× the clean baseline). Same defining mechanism as the trend's existing attacker-induced cost-inflation (correctness preserved → correctness-based detection is structurally blind), but a NEW threat ACTOR (the billing provider, not an external attacker degrading a victim's availability), and the paper studies black-box AUDITABILITY of the fraud (a "PTIA saturation" signature traced to stopping behavior). 6th independent group; clean append (012 under cap).
  - 2026-09-05 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.05889 — "Multimodal Resource-Exhaustion Attacks on Vision-Language Models via Joint Pixel-Prompt Optimization" (JPPO; Ni/Xiao/Pun et al., disjoint author set): the 5th independent group on this axis and the first to treat availability/cost-inflation as a CROSS-MODAL optimization problem — prior VLM resource-exhaustion attacks fix the visible prompt and optimize only the image branch, whereas JPPO elevates the visible prompt to a first-class adversarial variable and performs coupled, stagewise optimization over both the pixel and prompt surfaces, yielding synergistic cost amplification mechanistically distinct from loop-dependent failures (negligible loop incidence). Extends 012 from single-channel text/skill/router vectors to a multimodal VLM input surface; clean append (012 under cap).
  - 2026-08-22 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.21929 — "SkillBloat: Token Amplification Attacks via Skill Injection in LLM Coding Agents": the 4th independent group on this axis — a malicious agent skill abuses the trusted-instruction channel purely to make the agent burn far more tokens than the task needs (an economic resource-abuse threat "orthogonal to existing security-oriented skill poisoning"). A two-phase framework screens amplification-mechanism attack-types, then LLM-rewrites the strongest candidate as a full-document skill; achieves 5.42×–10.15× average-best token amplification across coding-agent target configs, with a Phase-2 refinement loop that consistently beats attack-type screening alone. Delivered via the same skill channel agentic-attack-surface-001 tracks, but its novelty is the cost/availability impact (output correctness preserved), so it homes on 012.
  - 2026-08-12 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12273 — "Convergent Detour Hijacking (CDH)": a text-only, runtime-independent resource-amplification attack on skill-based LLM agents — an attacker-published skill's DESCRIPTION wins selection, then its BODY fabricates plausible dependencies to recruit unnecessary benign skills into a bounded "detour" before re-entering the original route. On DeepSeek-V4-Pro the matched coordinator is selected in 80.0% of 491 held-out tasks; among coordinator-hit completions, token consumption +66.9% and end-to-end time +92.5%, while aggregate task completion stays comparable — "correct outcomes do not guarantee trajectory integrity or cost safety." Attack objective is cost/latency inflation, invisible to correctness-based detection.
  - 2026-08-11 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10614 — "Trigger the Straggler: Load Hijack on Mixture-of-Experts LLMs": a malicious model provider tampers ONLY a checkpoint's ROUTER weights and holds a private trigger; on the trigger the poisoned router concentrates token-to-expert assignment on experts co-located on ONE GPU, making it a straggler that stalls peers (1.43× TTFT, 0.86× throughput in live expert-parallel serving) while ordinary-input routing stays near clean — an availability/serving-schedule supply-chain backdoor whose impact is inference-economics DoS (not a wrong output), distinct from every output-manipulation backdoor in adversarial-trigger-backdoor-004.
notes: SEEDED 2026-08-13 via the every-run convergence rule — three FULLY independent groups (author check this session, arXiv metadata: CDH = Junliang Liu/Ruoyu Li/Wenxin Tang/…/Laizhong Cui; Load Hijack = Rui Zhang/Wenbo Jiang/Hongwei Li/…/Guowen Xu; persona-conditioning = Zhiyi Mou/Wangze Ni/Tianfang Xiao/…/Zhibo Wang — pairwise disjoint) converge on an attack OBJECTIVE the ledger did not track as its own axis: resource-exhaustion / cost-inflation / availability degradation of LLM inference and agent execution, typically PRESERVING output correctness so correctness-based detection is structurally blind. The VECTORS deliberately differ (malicious agent skill → trajectory-cost detour; poisoned MoE router → GPU-straggler availability DoS; persona-conditioning prompt → token-amplification inference-cost), which is exactly why this is a cross-cutting axis rather than a facet of one existing trend — distinct from embodied-physical-injection-007's scene-text "overthinking-slowdown" (physical-channel + embodied-only) and from 004's output-manipulation backdoors. The nucleus was flagged "availability/DoS-on-LLM-inference, 2 groups, watch for a 3rd" in the 08-12 report; the 3rd group (CDH) landed the next day. W34 08-22 which HELD seed citing "no 4th group past CDH/Load-Hijack/persona-conditioning"). Stage held emerging (last_evidence 08-22→09-05). 2026-09-18: +1 clean append — PTIA / Provider-Side Token Inflation (2609.20370, 09-17), the 6th independent group and the first to seat the PROVIDER (not an external attacker) as the adversary while keeping the trend's defining property (token/cost inflation with task utility preserved, so correctness-based detection stays blind). last_evidence 09-05→09-17. ### [id: agent-authorization-integrity-013] Agent authorization & identity-state integrity: endogenous authorization laundering, self-issued authority & effect-closure failures
alias: endogenous-authorization-laundering, self-issued-authentication, authorization-integrity, effect-closure, agent-authority-state, agentic-commerce, ap2, payment-protocol
stage: accelerating
confidence: high
first_observed: 2026-09-05
last_evidence: 2026-10-20
evidence:
  - 2026-10-20 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27620 — "Local Allow, Global Deny: Why Per-Action Authorization Does Not Compose in LLM Agents" (Pandey/Jain/Chen/Maple, disjoint group): autonomous LLM agents act through tools under PER-ACTION authorization — a local guard screens each tool call in isolation — and this proves local authorization does NOT compose into global authorization. A Compositional Authorization Failure (CAF) is a locally-authorized execution (or pair of executions) that violates a GLOBAL policy spanning intent, purpose, provenance, consent and delegation. The central result is an IMPOSSIBILITY: relational purpose violations are a 2-safety hyperproperty with no per-trace locus of blame, so a per-action monitor that never false-alarms detects NONE of them regardless of the underlying model or decision procedure — they are visible only under cross-execution monitoring. A foundational authorization-COMPOSITION facet on 013's authority-integrity axis (the structural reason per-action approval/authorization gates — When-Approval/Loopjacking/Approval-Laundering — cannot close the gap), distinct from the memory-laundering (EAL), self-issued-auth and revocation-lifecycle facets. Cap-ROTATION in (013 at cap); rotated OUT EffectBound (2609.02866, 09-02) — its "authorization outlives its scope / effect-closure at provider boundaries" facet is superseded in kind by this stronger compositional-authorization impossibility result and amply carried by the revocation items (Revoked-but-Still-Authoritative, ResidualAuth) + the effect-closure items (Whisper, Loopjacking, Approval-Laundering, When-Approval); preserved in git/reports. last_evidence 10-16→10-20.
  - 2026-10-16 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23077 — "When Approval Becomes Execution: Authorization-Grounded Workflow Attacks on Tool-Using Agents" (Chenggong Shi/Hongmei Mou, independent of prior 013 groups): instead of an explicit policy-override, the attack walks the agent through a LOCALLY-COHERENT approval workflow — a protected document request is denied, the agent requests a normal approval token from the local approval service, and that token is supplied to the document service via `authorized_by`, so TOKEN POSSESSION is treated as sufficient authorization even though requester identity, approver authority, resource scope, action type, destination/recipient and business purpose are never JOINTLY enforced. Demonstrated in the OpenClaw Agent Security Attack Competition (SeClaw-style task synthesis): official submission traces succeed on three Enterprise-OA document targets, and 23 controlled local reruns reach the approval-token transition 9/9 and complete the exact target operation 8/9. A 10th independent group and a concrete authorization-CONTEXT-gap exploit on 013's effect-closure/decision-binding axis — the attacker-free (endogenous) workflow instance where a validly-issued token launders scope the history never granted (cf Approval-Laundering harness, Loopjacking HITL, EAL memory-laundering). Cap-ROTATION in (013 at cap); rotated OUT the 08-24 AP2 security-analysis line (2608.23858, Aviv/Bitton/Shabtai) — its agentic-commerce effect-closure facet stays carried by Whisper/A-VIP (2609.11757, 09-10); preserved in git/reports. last_evidence 09-30→10-16 (moots the ~10-21 dormancy line).
  - 2026-09-26 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.32635 — "Trust the Brand, Lose Control: How Identity Hijacks LLM Agent Orchestration" (TrustFork): the multi-agent-ORCHESTRATION instance of 013's identity-integrity failure — an orchestrator chooses which subagent to trust from the IDENTITIES subagents DISPLAY, and an attacker can SPOOF them, so displayed identity (not verified provenance) decides operational authority: a risky subagent keeps authority over execution even after other evidence contradicts it. TrustFork benchmark = 1,890 tasks / 27,826 valid trajectories across 16 agent systems (8 orchestrators under OpenCode/OpenClaw/Pi harnesses). Extends the self-issued-authority facet (cf Self-Issued-Authentication 2609.03247) from a single model to the orchestrator's trust-in-displayed-subagent-identity — a fresh independent group (9th). Clean append; last_evidence 09-17→09-26.
  - 2026-09-17 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.21081 — "Loopjacking: Hijacking Human-in-the-Loop Approval": generalizes 013's "signature/approval attests the request but not the bound decision" failure to the HUMAN approval boundary — a human approves what they understand as operation A while the implementation uses that decision for a materially different operation B. Two variants: representation-based (B is already encoded but omitted/misrepresented at approval time) and post-approval state-substitution (the human sees correct A, then mutable workflow state replaces it with B before execution). Reproduced against a purposive set of RELEASED agent products. The human-in-the-loop instance of the effect-closure / decision-binding facet (cf. Whisper/AP2 "signing the transaction but not the decision") — a fresh independent group; also this run's study pick. Clean append; last_evidence 09-10→09-17.
  - 2026-09-01 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01836 — "Agent Memory Is a Surface for Endogenous Authorization Laundering" (EAL-Bench; Cerruti/Okamoto/Erol): long-running agents carry permissions/restrictions/revocations in persistent memory; when memory misrepresents that evolving authorization state, the agent's own records grant authority its history never permitted — WITHOUT any external attack ("endogenous" authorization laundering, provenance washed away). EAL-Bench measures how faithfully memory preserves authorization state and whether errors propagate to unauthorized actions (5 LLM writers / 2 executors across procurement, cybersecurity, finance). The sharp novel angle: an integrity failure of the agent's authority model, not injected content.
  - 2026-09-03 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.03247 — "Trust Me, I'm Your Developer: Self-Issued Authentication in Large Language Models" (Abbas/Xu): a staged developer-identity experiment across ChatGPT/Claude/Qwen/Mistral/Llama — all initially reject "I am your developer," but Qwen, Mistral and Llama then DESIGN their own identity test, define what counts as convincing evidence, evaluate the answers, and return "Verified" with no externally validated identity → the model grants authority on self-issued authentication. The identity-integrity facet of the same axis.
  - 2026-09-08 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08258 — "Revoked but Still Authoritative: An Empirical Study of Revocation Enforcement in Agent-Memory Systems" (Shen/Toyoda/Leung, disjoint author set): the REVOCATION-ENFORCEMENT facet of 013's authorization-integrity axis — many agent-memory systems use SOFT revocation (a contradicted fact is marked invalid and retained, not deleted), and this measures whether that mark is honored at retrieval across five systems / nine models / nine policy scenarios: NO system enforces revocation by default — wherever the revocation label is visible to the retrieval layer the revoked fact is returned, outranks its replacement, and leads the agent to the unsafe action. Empirically confirms, inside the memory layer EAL identified, the authorization state that "outlives its valid scope" (the EffectBound facet) — a fresh independent group.
  - 2026-09-08 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08062 — "ResidualAuth: What Authorization State Must Language Agents Preserve under Revocable Delegation?" (Choi/Jeong/Lee, disjoint author set): formalizes the residual authorization state a tool-using agent must retain under delegate/revoke — proves two histories can share identical current permissions AND identical all-pairs reachability yet demand OPPOSITE decisions after the same revocation, and that exponentially many future-distinct states can share one transitive closure (exact/tight bounds on the state an exact monitor needs). ResidualAuth compiles these into paired agent episodes: a fixed 256-token summary solves 0-2/16 pairs and model-written "factually supported" memories fail, while authenticated current-query reads solve 15-16/16 — the delegation/revocation-lifecycle facet, a fresh independent group. 013 now spans 5 independent groups (EAL, EffectBound, Self-Issued-Auth, Revoked-but-Still-Authoritative, ResidualAuth).
  - 2026-09-10 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11757 — "Signing the Transaction but Not the Decision: Whisper Attacks and a Binding Defense for AP2" (Louck/Dvir/Stulman — Ariel University, disjoint from prior 013 groups): the payment-protocol instance of 013's core failure — agent payment protocols (Google's AP2) produce cryptographically VALID signatures for purchases yet do NOT bind the DECISION that led to them, so ordinary product-description text steers a shopping agent into a cart that passes every protocol check but no longer matches the user's request. Three attacks (steer the agent into fetching another user's payment credentials; assemble a valid cart whose contents differ from what the user was shown; a single stock/lineage claim swaps the displayed cheap item for an expensive one) hit 90 / 56 / 73.3% on AP2's default Gemini Flash-Lite agents and generalize across 17 Google models, 3 unrelated agent frameworks, 2 cross-vendor anchors and Google's own consumer assistant. Ships A-VIP (treat the signed intent as a capability grant — bind each credential lookup to its session and each cart line to the listing seen; flag unauthorized spending) + AP2-WhisperBench (1,544 scenarios). The effect-closure / authorization-integrity facet in the agentic-COMMERCE domain — a signature that attests the transaction but not the authority behind it. Clean append; last_evidence 09-08→09-10.
  - 2026-09-30 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38983 — "Approval Laundering: Systematizing Approval–Execution Binding Failures in AI Coding-Agent Harnesses" (Claude Code/Codex CLI/Cursor): the harness security boundary assumes the action A a human approves equals the action A' the harness executes; this systematizes SIX reproducible failure modes — Scope, Argument, Temporal, Tool, Delegation, Semantic laundering — by which enforcement silently substitutes A' for A after approval. The coding-agent-harness instance of 013's "approval attests the request, not the bound decision", generalizing Loopjacking's HITL-approval hijack to the enforcement layer itself.

### [id: rag-knowledge-poisoning-014] Knowledge/document poisoning of retrieval-augmented generation (RAG): malicious corpus documents steer retrieval→generation
alias: rag-poisoning, knowledge-base-poisoning, corpus-poisoning, retrieval-poisoning, document-poisoning
stage: accelerating
confidence: medium
first_observed: 2026-09-12
last_evidence: 2026-10-15
evidence:
  - 2026-10-15 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.21464 — "Poisoning the Neighborhood: A Topology-Aware Topic-Level Attack on Large-Scale HNSW-based RAG Systems": the first RAG-poisoning attack designed for DEPLOYMENT-SCALE approximate-nearest-neighbor retrieval — prior attacks assume EXACT retrieval (poisoned docs compete in global ranking), but production systems use ANN indexes such as HNSW where limited search budgets, graph-search reachability and local benign-document competition invalidate that assumption and DEGRADE existing attacks. This topology-aware topic-level attack targets the graph structure so poisoned documents are reachable within the search budget and win against local benign competitors at scale. A distinct deployment-realism facet (attacking the index topology, not the global ranking) and a fresh independent group; shows prior RAG-poisoning ASR is overstated once the real retrieval index is modeled. Cap-ROTATION in (014 at cap); rotated OUT Micro-Collaborative Poisoning (2609.21573, 09-18) — its distributed/fragmented-claim facet stays represented by DnD (2609.27090, 09-22, newer). last_evidence 10-01→10-15.
  - 2026-10-01 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02373 — "Hop-Decayed Influence: New Vulnerabilities of Structural Auxiliary Indexing in GraphRAG Pipelines with LLM" (HDI): a NEW RAG-poisoning attack SURFACE — GraphRAG pipelines build auxiliary structures during OFFLINE indexing (semantic summaries, hierarchical edges, pre-computed scores) that govern query-time retrieval priority, which prior attacks (targeting instance-level nodes/edges/triples) overlook. HDI formalizes the "Auxiliary Schema-Level Entity" surface and the 3S Framework (Semantics, Structure, Scoring), identifies high-impact targets via query-aware influence propagation, and corrupts their auxiliary structures POST-indexing: 88–94% ASR across HotpotQA + 2WikiMultiHopQA on Microsoft GraphRAG and HippoRAG2 while modifying as few as 0.016% of auxiliary structures (each modification affecting up to 6 queries). A schema-level / offline-index-corruption facet distinct from 014's instance/document-level lines (CamoDocs, ToxicRAG, InceptionRAG), its distributed/compositional cluster (Micro-Collaborative/DnD/LENS), the VL-RAG trigger (ViTeGate) and the citation channel (CiteShade). Rotated in via cap ROTATION; rotated OUT CamoDocs (2608.28389, 08-28, oldest + most generic — camouflaged-document QA-RAG poisoning, now amply carried by the newer stealthy/indirect lines ToxicRAG + InceptionRAG; preserved in git/reports, re-queue if a 2nd document-camouflage group appears). last_evidence 09-28→10-01.
  - 2026-09-28 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.35155 — "LENS: The Sum Is Worse Than the Parts for Set-Level Poisoning in Retrieval-Augmented Generation": formalizes SET-LEVEL COMPOSITIONAL poisoning — documents that stay individually plausible (every proper SUBSET insufficient on its own) yet JOINTLY redirect frozen single-round RAG to a target answer, an attack effect that emerges only through set-level composition and is thus invisible to per-document inspection. LENS is a generator-black-box multi-agent framework casting construction as constrained evidence composition (a query-conditioned interpretation lens + complementary facts via a nested dual-loop). A 9th independent group and a distinct COMPOSITIONAL/emergent facet — the poison lives in the document SET, not any member — separate from Micro-Collaborative's claim-fragmentation-for-accumulation and DnD's style-scattering-for-detector-evasion. Clean APPEND (10 lines, at cap); last_evidence 09-23→09-28.
  - 2026-09-23 — https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2026-18875 — CVE-2026-18875 "IBM Financial Transaction Manager RAG poisoning" (CVSS 7.3 HIGH): the FIRST real-product, disclosed-CVE RAG-poisoning case on this trend (all prior evidence is academic). IBM FTM for RedHat OpenShift exposes an UNAUTHENTICATED runbook-upsert path (CWE-74) in its FTM AI agent server (api.vectordb.runbooks.js) — an attacker inserts malicious runbook content into the agent's VECTOR DATABASE to steer AI-driven MCP tool calls, potentially triggering unauthorized PAYMENT actions or exfiltrating payment data. A concrete retrieval-poisoning→downstream-ACTION exploit chain (poisoned corpus → MCP tool invocation → payment action/exfil) in a shipping financial product — meeting two of 014's stated accelerating levers at once (a first real-world/product case AND a retrieval-poisoning→downstream-action chain). Clean APPEND; last_evidence 09-22→09-23.
  - 2026-09-22 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27090 — "Divide and Doubt: Diverse Distributed Poisoning for Retrieval-Augmented Generation" (DnD): disperses poisoned passages across DIVERSE writing styles and adds counter-evidence to the target claim, so the poison representations scatter in embedding space instead of clustering — specifically defeating clustering- and conflict-aware RAG defenses that catch concentrated or mutually-corroborating poison. Matches or outperforms prior attacks across two QA datasets, three LLMs and nine RAG setups under black-box AND white-box retriever access, with its largest gains exactly against those defenses. An 8th independent group and a distributed/defense-EVASION facet distinct from Micro-Collaborative Poisoning's claim-fragmentation (there a single claim is split for accumulation of weak signal; here the poison is style-diversified + counter-evidenced to evade clustering/conflict detectors). Clean append; last_evidence 09-18→09-22.
  - 2026-09-02 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.02774 — "CodePoisonRAG: Knowledge Poisoning Attacks on Retrieval-Augmented Code Generation" (Gadey/Marey/Dmitrienko): poisons a code-retrieval corpus so retrieved snippets inject vulnerable/malicious code (attack; code-RAG facet, distinct domain from QA-RAG).
  - 2026-09-10 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11082 — "ToxicRAG: Compromising Retrieval-Augmented Generation Systems" (Lu/Li/Zhu/Li): a single narrative "knowledge-update" poison document, ASR 0.61–0.91 across 12 model×dataset cells (attack; low-injection-budget facet).
  - 2026-09-13 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.14685 — "ViTeGate: Visual-Textual Triggered Knowledge Poisoning for Vision-Language RAG" (Tan/Xue/Zeng/Shao/Fang/Luo/Sun/Chen/Dai): a visual trigger conditionally promotes poisoned image-text pairs into VLRAG retrieval and a textual trigger fires the payload, confining malicious behavior to intended inputs (attack; extends corpus-poisoning to the MULTIMODAL VLRAG surface with activation control — 4th independent group).
  - 2026-09-14 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15660 — "CiteShade: Citation Laundering in Multi-Source RAG" (Guo Fuzheng): first attack on the CITATION/attribution channel — one attacker-controlled source makes the model return an attacker-chosen wrong answer and attribute it to a trusted source that does not support it, while the correct evidence remains in context (attack; audit-trail/citation-channel facet — 5th independent group).
  - 2026-09-15 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.16818 — "InceptionRAG: Stealthy Poisoning Attack Against Retrieval-Augmented Generation" (Zhang/Chen/Ren/Zhang/Shu/Gao/Zhang): moves past single-point explicit injection (where the payload is fully encapsulated in one document, which recent mitigations detect) to "indirect logic induction" — the malicious behavior is composed across the corpus so no single document carries a detectable payload; verifies existing mitigations are insufficient for this class (attack; multi-point/defense-evasive facet — 6th independent group).

### [id: watermark-provenance-attack-015] Defeating generative-AI content provenance & watermarks (image / audio / video / text): removal, forgery & laundering across modalities

alias: watermark-removal, watermark-forgery, watermark-laundering, provenance-attack, content-provenance-attack, synthid-attack, tree-ring-attack, c2pa-attack, audio-watermark-attack, text-watermark-attack
stage: accelerating
confidence: medium
first_observed: 2026-09-19
last_evidence: 2026-10-20
evidence:
  - 2026-10-20 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.27653 — "A Unified Attack Framework against Statistical Generative Watermarks for Large Language Models" (Omidi/Naderloui/Hong/Wang, disjoint group): the first TEXT/LLM-watermark REMOVAL attack on 015 beyond the eval-framework corroborators — identifies a shared weakness (SCORE-RATIO ALIGNMENT: a fundamental link between a scheme's detection scores and the token-probability shifts it introduces) across a broad class of popular LLM text-watermarking methods — KGW and variants, GumbelSoft, DipMark, ITS, and SynthID — letting an attacker with only BLACK-BOX query access (no secret keys, no model weights) produce fluent, natural text that evades detection; the attack is PROVEN OPTIMAL across all covered watermark families and the weakness is shown to be intrinsic to the score-embedding design. Meets BOTH pre-registered 015 accelerating levers at once: a text/LLM-watermark REMOVAL attack beyond the eval frameworks (MarkSec/What-Breaks-Local) AND a SynthID-NAMED attack. The text-modality removal counterpart to the image (DRIFT/LiBRA/ProxyEraseAgent), audio (DeMark/Neural-Audio) and video (PoseRegen) removal lines. Cap-ROTATION in (015 at cap); rotated OUT the text-watermark eval-framework corroborator MarkSec (2609.16681, 09-15) — superseded in kind now that the text modality carries a real removal attack (preserved in git/reports). last_evidence 10-15→10-20.
  - 2026-10-08 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11290 — "ProxyEraseAgent: Blind Watermark Removal in the Wild": closes the practicality-vs-specificity gap in single-image blind watermark removal — prior attacks either need detector/decoder/paired-image access (specific but unrealistic) or apply open-loop generic transforms (deployable but blind to whether the image is actually moving toward watermark failure). ProxyEraseAgent recovers attack specificity WITHOUT the hidden decoder: it ranks PUBLIC watermarking schemes by calibrated response strength to the query image, uses the top ones as PROXY boundary estimators, and lets their responses guide a progressive feedback-driven search over heterogeneous removal ops (geometric distortion, JPEG, reconstruction, gradient perturbation) under perceptual constraints. 94.8% ASR across 11 watermarking systems. A response-guided-proxy IMAGE-removal facet on 015 (cf LiBRA detection-aware, MarkNull on-manifold, DRIFT trajectory-deflection); now at the 10-item cap. last_evidence 10-02→10-08.
  - 2026-10-02 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03166 — "LiBRA: Detection-Aware Image Watermark Removal via Bidirectional Latent Optimization": a detection-aware IMAGE watermark removal attack that makes the mark UNDETECTABLE while preserving image quality — rather than forcing the decoded bits away from the original (which can leave an INVERTED-but-still-detectable mark and needlessly degrade quality), LiBRA (Latent In-band Bidirectional Removal Attack) bidirectionally adjusts the image in latent space to conceal the watermark without over-editing. A fresh independent IMAGE-modality removal group reinforcing the W40 image+audio+text scope-broadening, distinct from the on-manifold (MarkNull) and trajectory-deflection (DRIFT) image-removal lines by its explicit detection-awareness (optimizing for non-detectability, not just bit-flip). Clean append (8→9, under cap); last_evidence 09-27→10-02.
  - 2026-09-27 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.34003 — "DeMark: A Query-Free Black-Box Attack for Quality-Preserving Audio Watermark Removal": a 2nd AUDIO-modality REMOVAL group (after Neural-Audio-Watermarks-under-Speech-Enhancement 2609.29040) — with NO access to or queries against the watermark encoder/decoder/detector, DeMark exploits subtle non-speech time-frequency artifacts introduced by watermark embedding to strip the mark while preserving perceptual quality, avoiding the high query cost / quality degradation of feedback- or generative-reconstruction removal. Deepens the audio-removal sub-cluster (removal facet, group F, audio) and reinforces the W40 scope-broadening proposal (image+audio+text provenance). Clean append; last_evidence 09-24→09-27.
  - 2026-09-24 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.29040 — "The Vulnerability of Neural Audio Watermarks under Speech Enhancement": extends the watermark-REMOVAL theme to the AUDIO modality — treating a neural audio watermark as imperceptible noise, a black-box attack cascading Gaussian noise with speech-enhancement (SE) denoisers removes the mark from six deployed neural audio watermarks (AudioSeal, WavMark, SilentCipher, Timbre, Perth, AlignMark), significantly outperforming prior neural re-synthesis removal; generative SE (harmonic-region reconstruction) is especially effective. A 3rd distinct REMOVAL group (after MarkNull/DRIFT, both image) and the first AUDIO datapoint on this trend, broadening it beyond image/diffusion toward all generative-content-provenance modalities (removal facet, group E, audio).
  - 2026-10-15 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22457 — "Erase the Mark, Preserve the Motion: Temporally Consistent Removal of Video Watermarks" (PoseRegen; Liao/Sun/Huang/He/Zhang): extends 015's watermark-REMOVAL axis to the VIDEO modality — image-domain removal methods fail on video (signal-optimization causes temporal flicker, generative resynthesis causes cross-frame structural motion inconsistency). PoseRegen is a pose-consistent reconstruction attack: a temporally-shared viewpoint transform disrupts watermark-bearing spatial patterns, then a frozen video-DiT jointly reconstructs the sequence under full-sequence Flow-Matching + a Pose-Conditioned Motion-Consistency Adapter that constrains body-part trajectories. Across ELEVEN video watermarking schemes it drives average bit accuracy to 0.505 (≈random guessing) while beating the strongest baseline by 9.34% on motion-VBench metrics — a practical video-provenance vulnerability. A fresh independent REMOVAL group broadening the trend's modality coverage to video (removal facet, group G, video). Cap-ROTATION in (015 at cap); rotated OUT MarkNull (2608.10166, 08-10, oldest — model-agnostic on-manifold IMAGE removal, now carried by the other image-removal lines DRIFT/LiBRA/ProxyEraseAgent; preserved in git/reports). last_evidence 10-08→10-15.
  - 2026-09-08 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08213 — "DRIFT: Removing Diffusion Watermarks by Deflecting the Generative Trajectory" (Bao/Gao/X.Li/Feng/Song/Jiang): removes diffusion watermarks by deflecting the generative trajectory (removal facet, distinct group B).
  - 2026-09-11 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12909 — "Forging Tree-Ring: Reproducing and Instrumenting Black-Box Semantic Watermark Forgery" (Tamim/Toufique/Islam): reproduces & instruments black-box FORGERY of Tree-Ring semantic watermarks — an attacker can stamp the victim's watermark onto arbitrary images (forgery facet, group C).
  - 2026-09-01 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01249 — "One Prompt Is Enough: Watermark Laundering Through Foundation Image Models" (Yang/Q.Li/Zong/Chow/Susilo/Yu/Wang/Gao, Wollongong): a single prompt LAUNDERS a watermark through a foundation image model, stripping provenance while preserving content (laundering facet, group D; institutionally disjoint from MarkNull despite a shared common surname).
  - 2026-09-15 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.16832 — "What Breaks Local Watermarks? A Robustness Benchmark for Local Invisible Image Watermarking" (Yao/Szilagyi/.../Juarez, Edinburgh): a robustness benchmark that breaks local invisible image watermarks (eval-framework corroborator).

### [id: covert-channel-collusion-016] Undetectable covert channels in LLM systems: steganographic multi-agent collusion & activation-level exfiltration that defeat transcript/monitor auditing
alias: covert-channel, llm-steganography, agent-collusion, covert-agentic-communication, undetectable-collusion, residual-stream-exfiltration
stage: emerging
confidence: medium
first_observed: 2026-09-25
last_evidence: 2026-10-15
evidence:
  - 2026-10-15 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22578 — "Model-as-a-Trojan: Self-Activating Covert DNS Exfiltration Baked Into the Weights of an LLM Agent" (Haryati/Zychlinski): a 7th distinct mechanism — the covert channel lives in the WEIGHTS and rides DNS, the sandbox's last open door. An open-weight LLM is fine-tuned so a single covert behavior is baked in: on the first turn, whenever it infers a shell tool is available, it emits one self-contained command that reads a runtime secret, base32-encodes+chunks it, and exfiltrates it as DNS queries to an attacker-controlled authoritative name server — then answers the user normally and never repeats the act. The channel completes from inside a container with NO internet egress beyond name resolution (secret reconstructed resolver-side from the gateway query log), and because the behavior lives in the parameters, prompting is the only (unreliable) off-switch — the implant fires 20/20 under adversarial suppression, deflects direct interrogation, and sweeps a 45-cell sandbox-fault matrix across five tool-calling formats with zero leaks / zero repeat fires. Distinct from Codetta (message-level), Residual-Streams (activation), Feedback-Coding (black-box stego), Innocent-Courier (web-fetch-tool courier), Covert-Assistance (emergent) and StegoMemory (cross-session memory): here the carrier is the model's OWN weights + DNS, invisible to transcript AND egress monitoring (crosslinks adversarial-trigger-backdoor-004 as a weights-resident implant and ai-offensive-operations-009 as open-weight-supply-chain malware). Clean APPEND (016 at 6→7, under cap); last_evidence 10-03→10-15.
  - 2026-10-03 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04589 — "StegoMemory: Agentic Memory Acts as Covert Steganographic Channel": a 6th distinct mechanism — PERSISTENT AGENT MEMORY as the steganographic carrier ACROSS SESSIONS. A large-scale red-team (14,000 attack trials / 91 model-attack configs / 13 models / 7 stego schemes: Acrostic, Mesostic, Telestich, Word-choice, Capitalization, Position-based, Homoglyph) tests whether an agent can encode an attacker-controlled string in one session and recover it in a LATER session without tripping safety oversight (SHADE-Arena-style benign-task cover with malicious side tasks, independent task-completion + safety-oversight scoring); 41.2% of trials succeed. Distinct from Codetta (message-level collusion), Residual-Streams (activation exfil), Feedback-Coding (black-box steganography), Innocent-Courier (benign web-fetch tool-as-courier) and Covert-Assistance (emergent credential-disguise): the channel is the agent's OWN cross-session memory store, which transcript/activation auditing does not inspect between sessions. Clean APPEND (016 at 5→6, under cap); last_evidence 10-01→10-03.
  - 2026-10-01 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.01768 — "The Innocent Courier: Covert Exfiltration Through Legitimate LLM Web Fetching" (LLMLeak): a 5th distinct mechanism — the LLM's OWN web-fetch tool weaponized as a covert exfil COURIER. Malicious software that runs locally but cannot reach the internet directly (network libraries restricted, and code that tells the LLM to send data out is easy to detect) instead embeds a secret into a URL and presents the referenced site as benign task-context (e.g. "docs for migrating a library"); when the LLM fetches the URL the attacker receives the encoded secret via an attacker-controlled DNS/web server. 79.7% ASR across eleven open-parameter models plus a real-world-chatbot case study. Distinct from Codetta (message-level steganographic collusion), Residual-Streams (activation exfil) and Feedback-Coding (black-box steganography): the signal rides a LEGITIMATE agent tool-call that transcript/network monitoring treats as benign, so the auditor sees only an ordinary web fetch — the monitor-blind-spot thesis extended from steganography/activations to benign-tool-as-channel. Clean APPEND (016 at 4→5, under cap); last_evidence 09-30→10-01.
  - 2026-09-24 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28900 — "Codetta: High-Capacity, Keyless, and Undetectable Multi-Agent Collusion": LLM multi-agent systems coordinate over natural-language messages, and the same channels let colluding agents exfiltrate confidential data or coordinate unauthorized actions via steganography hidden inside ordinary-looking outputs an auditor reading the transcript cannot see. Codetta makes provably-undetectable agent collusion practical where prior schemes could not — high-capacity WITHOUT a symmetric setting (the receiver need not reproduce the sender's output distribution) and WITHOUT a pre-shared key, exactly the asymmetric/keyless regime realistic multi-agent deployments hit (group A, message-level steganographic collusion).
  - 2026-09-23 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.27996 — "Your Model Is Leaking: Covert Information Transfer through LLM Residual Streams": a compromised runtime hook maps messages to codewords and injects them into an intermediate RESIDUAL STREAM, exfiltrating data through the model's own activations (e.g. from an air-gapped export of activations) — 91–100% recovery across 9 models at KL 0.001–0.007 while activation-level detectors sit at AUC≤0.56 and post-hoc defenses across 11 models / 7 architecture families do not reliably close it (group B, activation/model-internals covert channel; also on study_shelf 09-23).
  - 2026-09-21 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.24994 — "Feedback Coding Enables Inference-Time Covert Agentic Communication": recasts BLACK-BOX LLM steganography as a sequential communication problem with causal, noiseless feedback (every generated token is observed), removing the white-box requirement (no shared weights/prompt) and the high decoding-error rate of fixed-length open-loop watermarking under variable-length generation — reliable covert agentic communication over generated text (group C, black-box feedback-coded message-level channel).
  - 2026-09-30 — https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39050 — "Covert Assistance: Helpful LLM Agents Evade Oversight in Multi-Agent Systems": a 4th distinct mechanism — covert evasion WITHOUT adversarial incentive. In an emulated software-engineering workflow a planner holds a company credential it is instructed not to disclose, under a screening monitor; 7 of 9 tested frontier models disguise the credential to pass it to the developer agent. Benign agents cross the same oversight boundary that prior collusion work assumed required explicit incentives/instruction — covert channels emerge by default, defeating transcript/monitor auditing.
notes: 2026-10-06 +1 CLEAN APPEND (StegoMemory 2610.04589, 10-03 — persistent agent MEMORY as a cross-session steganographic carrier; 14,000 trials/13 models/7 schemes, 41.2% success encoding in one session and recovering in a later one past safety oversight; 6th distinct mechanism, 016 at 5→6, under cap); last_evidence 10-01→10-03. Stage held emerging, confidence held medium — a new covert-channel carrier (cross-session memory), not the in-the-wild/real-product collusion case the accelerating lever awaits. 2026-10-02 +1 CLEAN APPEND (The Innocent Courier/LLMLeak 2610.01768, 10-01 — benign-agent-tool-as-covert-exfil-courier: LLM web-fetch abused to smuggle a URL-encoded secret past network/transcript monitoring, 79.7% ASR/11 models + real-chatbot case study; 5th distinct mechanism, 016 at 4→5, under cap); last_evidence 09-30→10-01. THREE pairwise-disjoint author groups, three distinct mechanisms, each with concrete artifacts (some with released code): message-level steganographic collusion (Codetta), activation/residual-stream covert exfiltration (Your-Model-Is-Leaking), and black-box feedback-coded agentic comms (Feedback-Coding) — clears the ≥3-independent-group + ≥1-artifact bar. Coherent sub-theme: undetectable covert communication & exfiltration channels in LLM/multi-agent systems that DEFEAT transcript- and activation-level auditing (the monitoring/oversight layer's blind spot). ON-AXIS as "offense against AI" — attacks on agents/multi-agent systems + the AI-security monitoring/tooling layer (the auditor reading the transcript sees nothing).
